## I Lotus World / I 莲花世界

===Original===
There was a shading of rose in the pearl arch of sky, deepening at the horizon meeting of sea and air in a rainbow tint of cloud. The lazy swells of the ocean held the same soft color, darkened with crimson veins where spirals of weed drifted. A rose world bathed in soft sunlight, knowing only gentle winds, peace, and﻿—sloth.

Ross Murdock leaned forward over the edge of the rock ledge to peer down at a beach of fine sand, pale pink sand with here and there a glitter of a crystalline “shell”﻿—or were those delicate, fluted ovals shells? Even the waves came in languidly. And the breeze which ruffled his hair, smoothed about his sun-browned, half-bare body, caressed it, did not buffet on its way inland to stir the growths which the Terran settlers called “trees” but which possessed long lacy fronds instead of true branches.

Hawaika﻿—named for the old Polynesian paradise﻿—a world seemingly without flaw except the subtle one of being too perfect, too welcoming, too wooing. Its long, uneventful, unchanging days enticed forgetfulness, offered a life without effort. Except for the mystery.﻿ ﻿…

Because this world was not the one pictured on the tape which had brought the Terran settlement team here. A map, a directing guide, a description all in one, that was the ancient voyage tape. Ross himself had helped to loot a storehouse on an unknown planet for a cargo of such tapes. Once they had been the space-navigation guides for a race or races who had ruled the star lanes ten thousand years in his own world’s past, a civilization which had long since sunk again into the dust of its beginning.

===Chinese===
珍珠色穹顶般的天空中透着一抹玫瑰色泽，到海天相接的地平线处渐深，化作一抹虹彩的云。慵懒的海浪涌动着同样柔和的色彩，螺旋状漂流的海藻所过之处，深出一道道绛红的脉络。这是一个浸润在柔和阳光里的玫瑰色世界，只识得微风、安宁，以及——怠惰。

罗斯·默多克（Ross Murdock）从岩台边缘探身向前，俯瞰脚下的细沙海滩——浅粉色的沙地上，星星点点闪着结晶质「贝壳」的微光——可那些精致的、带凹槽的椭圆当真是贝壳吗？就连海浪也懒洋洋地涌上岸来。微风撩起他的头发，又滑过他被太阳晒成古铜色的半裸身躯，温柔地爱抚着，一路向内陆去时不曾半点呼啸肆虐，只轻轻拂动那些被特兰殖民者称作「树」的植物——它们没有真正的枝干，只披垂着长长的蕾丝般叶片。

哈瓦伊卡（Hawaika）——得名于古老的波利尼西亚乐园传说——一个看上去毫无瑕疵的世界，唯一的暗瑕是它太完美、太殷勤、太魅惑。漫长、平淡、一成不变的日子引人遗忘，奉上一种无需劳作的生活。只是，还有那个谜。……

因为这颗星球，并不是把特兰殖民队带到此处的那条磁带上描绘的世界。地图、导航指南、志书，三合一——那便是远古的航行磁带。罗斯本人就曾参与洗劫一颗无名星球上的库房，运回整整一批这样的磁带。它们曾是一个或数个种族的星际导航指南——那个文明曾在罗斯自己世界的一万年前统御星海航道，如今早已重新沉入其发轫之时的尘埃。

===Original===
Those tapes returned to Terra after their chance discovery, were studied, probed, deciphered by the best brains of his time, shared out by lot between already suspicious Terran powers, bringing into the exploration of space bitter rivalries and old hatreds.

Such a tape had landed their ship on Hawaika, a world of shallow seas and archipelagoes instead of true continents. The settlement team had had all the knowledge contained on that tape crowded into them, only to discover that much they had learned from it was false!

Of course, none of them had expected to discover here still the cities, the civilization the tape had projected as existing in that long-ago period. But no present island string they had visited approximated those on the maps they had seen, and so far they had not found any trace that any intelligent beings had walked, built, lived, on these beautiful, slumberous atolls. So, what had happened to the Hawaika of the tape?

===Chinese===
那些磁带被偶然发现后送回特拉（地球），由他那个时代最聪明的头脑研究、探测、破译，再由抽签分给本就互相猜忌的各特兰强国，把苦涩的对抗与宿怨一并带进了太空探索。

正是这样一条磁带，把他们的飞船引导降落到了哈瓦伊卡——一个只有浅海与群岛、没有真正大陆的世界。殖民队员们曾被强行灌输磁带上记载的全部知识，结果却发现，他们从中学到的许多东西竟是错的！

当然，谁也没指望能在这里发现磁带所投射的那个遥远年代尚存的城市与文明。可他们走访过的岛链，没有一条与他们看过的地图上的相符；时至今日，他们也没在这些美丽而沉睡的环礁上找到丝毫痕迹，证明曾有智慧生命在这里行走、建造、生活过。那么，磁带上那个哈瓦伊卡，究竟出了什么事？

===Original===
Ross’s right hand rubbed across the ridged scars which disfigured his left one, to be carried for the rest of his life as a mark of his meeting with the star voyagers in the past of his own world. He had deliberately seared his own flesh to break the mental control they had asserted. Then the battle had gone to him. But from it he had brought another scar﻿—the unease of that old terror when Ross Murdock, fighter, rebel, outlaw by the conventions of his own era, Ross Murdock who considered himself an exceedingly tough individual, that toughness steeled by the training for Time Agent sorties, had come up against a power he did not understand, instinctively hated and feared.

Now he breathed deeply of the wind﻿—the smell of the sea, the scents of the land growths, strange but pleasant. So easy to relax, to drop into the soft, lulling swing of this world in which they had found no fault, no danger, no irritant. Yet, once those others had been here﻿—the blue-suited, hairless ones he called “Baldies.” And what had happened then﻿ ﻿… or afterward?

A black head, brown shoulders, slender body, broke the sleepy slip of the waves. A shimmering mask covered the face, catching glitter-fire in the sun. Two hands freed a chin curved yet firmly set, a mouth made more for laughter than sternness, wide dark eyes. Karara Trehern of the Alii, the onetime Hawaiian god-chieftain line, was an exceedingly pretty girl.

===Chinese===
罗斯的右手摩挲着左手背上疙瘩嶙峋的疤——那是他与星际航行者在自己世界的过去相遇留下的印记，要伴随他余生。当年为了挣脱对方施加的精神控制，他曾毅然烧灼自己的血肉。那一战最终是他赢了。可他也从中学来了另一道疤——那种古老恐惧留下的不安：当年，罗斯·默多克——这个按他所处时代的规矩算得上斗士、叛逆、法外之徒的罗斯·默多克，这个自认相当强悍、又经时间特工出击训练淬炼得愈发坚韧的汉子——撞上了一种他无法理解、本能地憎恨与畏惧的力量。

此刻他深深吸了一口风——海的气息，陆上植物的芬芳，陌生却宜人。在这颗找不到一丝缺陷、一点危险、一样刺激的星球上，放松下来何其容易，何其容易就沉入它那温柔催眠的摇曳节律。然而，那些「别人」曾经来过——那群一身蓝衣、没有毛发的家伙，他称之为「秃头人」（Baldies）。后来出了什么事……再后来呢？

一颗乌黑的头颅、一副棕色的肩膀、一个纤细的身影，破开了睡意沉沉的微波。一张微光闪烁的面罩遮着脸，在日光下捕着点点流火。两只手摘下面罩，露出线条弧柔却咬合坚定的下巴，一张生来宜笑不宜嗔的嘴，一双又大又黑的眼睛。出身阿利伊（Alii）——昔日夏威夷神王世系——的卡拉拉·特雷赫恩（Karara Trehern），是个漂亮得出众的姑娘。

===Original===
But Ross regarded her aloofly, with a coldness which bordered on hostility, as she flipped her mask into its pocket on top of the gill-pack. Below his rocky perch she came to a halt, her feet slightly apart in the sand, an impish twist to her lips as she called mockingly:

“Why not come in? The water’s fine.”

“Perfect, like all the rest of this.” Some of his impatience came out in the sour tone. “No luck, as usual?”

===Chinese===
可罗斯冷眼看着她，那冷淡近乎敌意，这时她正把面罩啪地塞进人工鳃背包（gill-pack）顶端的口袋。在他那处岩栖下方，她停住脚步，双脚在沙地里微微分开，唇边挂着一丝淘气的弯弧，扬声打趣道：

「怎么不下来？水里舒服得很。」

「舒服得很，跟这颗星球上的一切一样。」他的不耐烦从酸溜溜的语气里漏出来几分。「还是老样子，一无所获？」

===Original===
“As usual,” Karara conceded. “If there ever was a civilization here, it’s been gone so long we’ll probably never find any traces. Why don’t you just pick out a good place to set up that time-probe and try it blind?”

Ross scowled. “Because”﻿—his patience was exaggerated to the point of insult﻿—“we have only one peep-probe. Once it’s set we can’t tear it down easily for transport somewhere else, so we want to be sure there’s something to look at beyond.”

She began to wring the water out of her long hair. “Well, as far as we’ve explored﻿ ﻿… nothing. Come yourself next time. Tino-rau and Taua aren’t particular; they like company.”

===Chinese===
「老样子。」卡拉拉认了。「这里就算真有过文明，也消失得太久，我们八成永远找不到任何痕迹了。你何不干脆挑个好地方，把那台时间探测器（time-probe）架起来，闭着眼睛盲试一把？」

罗斯皱起眉。「因为」——他的耐心被拉长到了近乎侮辱的地步——「我们只有一台窥视探头（peep-probe）。一旦架好，就没法轻易拆下来运去别处，所以我们得先确定底下真有东西可看。」

她开始拧干长发里的水。「反正，我们探到的地方……什么都没有。下次你自己来吧。蒂诺-劳（Tino-rau）和塔乌亚（Taua）不挑人，它们喜欢有伴儿。」

===Original===
Putting two fingers to her mouth, Karara whistled. Twin heads popped out of the water, facing the shore and her. Projecting noses, mouths with upturned corners so they curved in a lasting pleasant grin at the mammals on the shore﻿—the dolphin pair, mammals whose ancestors had chosen the sea, whistled back in such close counterfeit of the girl’s signal that they could be an echo of her call. Years earlier their species’ intelligence had surprised, almost shocked, men. Experiments, training, cooperation, had developed a tie which gave the water-limited race of mankind new eyes, ears, minds, to see, evaluate, and report concerning an element in which the bipeds were not free.

Hand in hand with that cooperation had gone other experiments. Just as the clumsy armored diving suits of the early twentieth century had allowed man to begin penetration into a weird new world, so had the frogman equipment made him still freer in the sea. And now the gill-pack which separated the needed oxygen from the water made even that lighter burden of tanks obsolete. But there remained depths into which man could not descend, whose secrets were closed to him. There the dolphins operated, in a partnership of minds, equal minds﻿—though that last fact had been difficult for man to accept.

Ross’s irritation, unjustified as he knew it to be, did not rest on Tino-rau or Taua. He enjoyed the hours when he buckled on gill-pack and took to the sea with those two ten-foot, black-and-silver escorts sharing the action. But Karara﻿ ﻿… Karara’s presence was a different matter altogether.

===Chinese===
卡拉拉把两根手指抵在唇边，吹了声口哨。两颗脑袋从水里冒出来，正对着海岸和她。前突的口鼻、嘴角上弯的嘴，冲着岸上的哺乳动物绽出持久的、讨喜的笑——这对海豚，这对祖先选择了海洋的哺乳动物，回了一声口哨，学得跟姑娘的信号惟妙惟肖，简直可以算她呼唤的回声。早些年，它们这个种族的智力曾让人类惊讶，甚至近乎震惊。实验、训练、合作，发展出一种纽带，让在水中受限的人类种族获得了新的眼睛、耳朵和头脑，去观看、评估并汇报那种两足动物无法自由驰骋的介质中的情形。

与这种合作携手并进的还有其他实验。正如二十世纪初笨重的盔甲式潜水服让人类得以初探一个奇异的崭新世界，蛙人装备又让他在海中愈发自由。而如今，能从水中分离出所需氧气的人工鳃背包，连气瓶这副较轻的负担也一并淘汰了。但人类仍有下不去的深度，那里的秘密对他紧闭着。海豚便在那样的深处活动，与人结成心智上的伙伴关系，对等的心智——尽管这最后一点，人类曾很难接受。

罗斯心头的烦躁，尽管他自己也知道没什么道理，却不是冲着蒂诺-劳或塔乌亚来的。他喜欢背上人工鳃背包、带着那两位十英尺长、黑银相间的护卫一同下海分享行动的时光。可卡拉拉……卡拉拉的存在，完全是另一回事。

===Original===
The Agents’ teams had always been strictly masculine. Two men partnered for an interlocking of abilities and temperaments, going through training together, becoming two halves of a strong and efficient whole. Before being summarily recruited into the Project, Ross had been a loner﻿—living on the ragged edges of the law, an indigestible bit for the civilization which had become too ordered and “adjusted” to absorb his kind. But in the Project he had discovered others like himself﻿—men born out of time, too ruthless, too individualistic for their own age, but able to operate with ease in the dangerous paths of the Time Agents.

And when the time search for the wrecked alien ships had succeeded and the first intact ship found, used, duplicated, the Agents had come from forays into the past to be trained anew for travel to the stars. First there had been Ross Murdock, criminal. Then there had been Ross Murdock and Gordon Ashe, Time Agents. Now there was still Ross and Gordon and a quest as perilous as any they had known. Yet this time they had to depend upon Karara and the dolphins.

“Tomorrow”﻿—Ross was still not sorting out his thoughts, truly aware of the feeling which worked upon him as a thorn in the finger﻿—“I will come.”

===Chinese===
时间特工的小队向来清一色是男性。两人搭档，能力与脾性互补咬合，一同受训，成为一个强大高效的整体的两半。在被半强制招募进「计划」（the Project）之前，罗斯一直独来独往——游走在法律的破烂边缘，是那个变得太井然、太「调适」而容不下他这种人的文明消化不了的一粒砂。但在「计划」里，他发现了别的同类——一群生错时代的人，对他们自己的时代而言太狠辣、太个人主义，却能在时间特工的凶险路径上行走自如。

而当穿越时间搜寻外星失事飞船的行动成功、第一艘完好的飞船被找到、启用、复制之后，特工们从过去的突袭中抽身出来，重新受训，准备远航群星。起初，只有罗斯·默多克，一个罪犯。后来，有了罗斯·默多克和戈登·阿什（Gordon Ashe），一对时间特工。如今依然是罗斯和戈登，还有一场不逊于以往任何一次的凶险远征。只不过这一回，他们得依靠卡拉拉和海豚了。

「明天」——罗斯还没理清自己的思绪，还没真正看清那枚如扎在指尖的木刺般隐隐作痛的情绪到底是什么——「我来。」

===Original===
“Good!” If she recognized his hostility for what it was, that did not bother her. Once more she whistled to the dolphins, waved a casual farewell with one hand, and headed up the beach toward the base camp. Ross chose a more rugged path over the cliff.

Suppose they did not find what they sought near here? Yet the old taped map suggested that this was approximately the site starred upon it. Marking a city? A star port?

Ashe had volunteered for Hawaika, demanded this job after the disastrous Topaz affair when the team of Apache volunteers had been sent out too soon to counter what might have been a Red sneak settlement. Ross was still unhappy over the ensuing months when only Major Kelgarries and maybe, in a lesser part, Ross had kept Gordon Ashe in the Project at all. That Topaz had been a failure was accepted when the settlement ship did not return. And that had added to Ashe’s sense of guilt for having recruited and partially trained the lost team.

===Chinese===
「好极了！」就算她认出了那敌意的真面目，也毫不在意。她又朝海豚吹了声口哨，一只手随意挥了挥算作道别，便沿着沙滩朝基地营地去。罗斯则挑了一条更崎岖的路，翻崖而去。

倘若他们在附近找不到要找的东西呢？可那条古老的磁带地图表明，这里大致就是图上标星的位置。标的是一座城市？还是一座星际港？

阿什是自愿报名来哈瓦伊卡的，是那场灾难性的托帕兹（Topaz）事件之后主动讨来的差事——当时那支阿帕奇志愿队被过早派了出去，去对抗一处可能是红方（Reds）偷建的定居点。此后那几个月，全靠凯尔加里斯少校（Major Kelgarries）、或许再加上出力稍逊的罗斯，才把戈登·阿什留在了「计划」里，罗斯至今想起仍不痛快。殖民船一去不返，托帕兹行动的失败便成了定论。这又给阿什的负罪感添了一层——毕竟那支失落的队伍是他招募并训练过一阵的。

===Original===
Among those dispatched over Ashe’s vehement protests had been Travis Fox who had shared with Ashe and Ross the first galactic flight in an age-old derelict spaceship. Travis Fox﻿—the Apache archaeologist﻿—had he ever reached Topaz? Or would he and his team wander forever between worlds? Did they set down on a planet where some inimical form of native life or a Red settlement had awaited them? The very uncertainty of their fate continued to ride Ashe.

So he insisted on coming out with the second settlement team, the volunteers of Samoan and Hawaiian descent, to carry on a yet more exciting and hazardous exploration. Just as the Project had probed into the past of Terra, so would Ashe and Ross now attempt to discover what lay in the past of Hawaika, to see this world as it had been at the height of the galactic civilization, and so to learn what they could about their forerunners into space. And the mystery they had dropped into upon landing added to the necessity for that discovery or discoveries.

Their probe, if fortune favored them, might become a gate through time. The installation was a vast improvement over these passage points they had first devised. Technical information had taken a vast leap forward after Terran engineers and scientists had had access to the tapes of the stellar empire. Adaptations and shortcuts developed, so that a new hybrid technology came into use, woven from the knowledge and experimentation of two civilizations thousands of years apart in time.

===Chinese===
在阿什的激烈抗议之下仍被派出去的人里，有特拉维斯·福克斯（Travis Fox），他曾与阿什和罗斯一起，乘着一艘上古的废弃飞船完成过首次银河系飞行。特拉维斯·福克斯——那位阿帕奇考古学家——他到底到没到托帕兹？还是说，他会和他的队伍永远漂流在群星之间？他们是不是降落在了某颗行星上，那里有怀有敌意的土著生命，或是一处红方定居点正等着他们？正是这种命运的不确定，一直骑在阿什心头挥之不去。

于是他坚持随第二支殖民队出来——一支由萨摩亚和夏威夷后裔的志愿者组成的队伍——去继续一场更为刺激也更加凶险的探索。正如「计划」曾探入特拉的过去，如今阿什和罗斯也要试着发掘哈瓦伊卡的过去，看看这颗星球在银河文明鼎盛之时是什么模样，从而尽可能了解那些先行进入太空的先驱们。而他们一降落便跌进的那团谜，更让这发现——或那一系列发现——势在必行。

他们的探测器，若得天眷顾，或许能成为一道贯通时间之门。这套装置比起他们最初设计的那些通行点，已是长足的进步。自特兰的工程师和科学家得以接触恒星帝国的磁带之后，技术知识突飞猛进。各种改造与捷径接连问世，一种新的混合技术由此投入使用——它由两个在时间上相隔数千年的文明的知识与实验交织而成。

===Original===
If and when he or Ashe﻿—or Karara and her dolphins﻿—discovered the proper site, the two Agents could set up their own equipment. Both Ross and Ashe had had enough drill in the process. All they needed was the brick of discovery; then they could build their wall. But they must find some remainder of the past, the smallest trace of ancient ruin upon which to center their peep-probe. And since landing here the long days had flowed into weeks with no such discovery made.

Ross crossed the ridge of rock which formed a cockscomb rise on the island’s spine and descended to the village. As they had been trained, the Polynesian settlers adapted native products to their own heritage of building and tools. It was necessary that they live off the land, for their transport ship had had storage space only for a limited number of supplies and tools. After it took off to return home they would be wholly on their own for several years. Their ship, a silvery ball, rested on a rock ledge, its pilot and crew having lingered to learn the results of Ashe’s search. Four days more and they would have to lift for home even if the Agents still had only negative results to report.

That disappointment was driving Ashe, the way that six months earlier his outrage and guilt feelings over the Topaz affair had driven him. Karara’s suggestion carried weight the longer Ross thought about it. With more swimmers hunting, there was just that much increased chance of turning up some clue. So far the dolphins had not reported any dangerous native sea life or any perils except the natural ones any diver always had at his shoulder under the waves.

===Chinese===
只要他或阿什——或者卡拉拉和她的海豚——找到了合适的地点，两位特工就能架起自己的设备。罗斯和阿什对这套流程都操练得够多了。他们缺的只是发现这块砖；有了砖，便能砌起墙。可他们必须找到某种过去的残迹，哪怕是最微小的一星半点古废墟，好让窥视探头有个聚焦的中心。而自从降落此地，长长的日子已流成了一个个星期，这样的发现却连一丝影子也没有。

罗斯翻过岛脊上那道状如鸡冠的岩棱，下到村子里。波利尼西亚殖民者按受训所学，把当地物产改造成他们世代传下的建筑与工具样式。他们必须靠这片土地过活，因为运输船的储物空间只装得下有限的补给和工具。等它起飞返航之后，他们就要完完全全自力更生好几年。他们的飞船——一颗银色的圆球——停在一处岩台上，飞行员和船员留了下来，等着看阿什搜索的结果。再过四天，无论特工们汇报的是不是依旧一无所获，他们都必须升空回家。

这份失望正驱策着阿什，一如半年前托帕兹事件引发的愤懑与负疚驱策着他。罗斯越琢磨，越觉得卡拉拉的提议有分量。下水搜寻的人越多，翻出线索的机会就相应大上几分。到目前为止，海豚们除了每个潜水者在浪下随时会遭遇到的那些天然险情之外，还没有报告过任何危险的土著海洋生物，也没有任何别的凶险。

===Original===
There were extra gill-packs, and all of the settlers were good swimmers. An organized hunt ought to shake the Polynesians out of their present do-it-tomorrow attitude. As long as they had had definite work before them﻿—the unloading of the ship, the building of the village, all the labors incidental to the establishing of this base﻿—they had shown energy and enthusiasm. It was only during the last couple of weeks that the languor which appeared part of the atmosphere here had crept up on them, so that now they were content to live at a slower and lazier pace. Ross remembered Ashe’s comparison made the evening before, likening Hawaika to a legendary Terran island where the inhabitants lived a drugged existence, feeding upon the seeds of a native plant. Hawaika was fast becoming a lotus land for Terrans.

“Through here, then westward.﻿ ﻿…” Ashe hunched over the crate table in the mat-walled house. He did not look up as Ross entered. Karara’s still damp head was bowed until those black locks, now sleeked to her round skull, almost touched the man’s close-cropped brown hair. They were both studying a map as if they saw not lines on paper but the actual inlets and lagoons which that drawing represented.

“You are sure, Gordon, that this *is* the modern point to match the site on the tape?” The girl brushed back straying hair.

===Chinese===
人工鳃背包有多余的，殖民者又个个都是游泳好手。一场有组织的搜寻，正可以把波利尼西亚人从眼下这种「明日复明日」的做派里震醒。只要面前摆着明确的活计——卸船、建村、建立这个基地的一切杂务——他们向来不乏干劲和热情。偏偏是最近这两个星期，那种仿佛融在这方空气里的怠惰悄悄爬上了他们的身子，如今他们竟安于过得更慢、更懒了。罗斯想起阿什前一晚打的比方：他把哈瓦伊卡比作传说中的一座特拉海岛，岛上居民靠吃一种本地植物的种子过活，终日浑浑噩噩如坠迷梦。哈瓦伊卡正在迅速变成特兰人的食莲之乡。

「从这儿穿过去，然后向西。……」阿什弓着身子，伏在草墙屋里的板条箱桌上。罗斯进来时，他头也没抬。卡拉拉还湿着的头低垂着，一头黑发服帖地拢在圆颅上，几乎碰到男人剪得极短的棕色头发。两人都在研究一张地图，仿佛纸上呈现的不是线条，而是那幅图所代表的真实海湾与潟湖。

「戈登，你确定这*就*是能与磁带上那个地点对上的现代方位？」姑娘拂开散落的发丝。

===Original===
Ashe shrugged. There were tight brackets about his mouth which had not been there six months ago. He moved jerkily, not with the fluid grace of those old days when he had faced the vast distance of time travel with unruffled calm and a self-confidence to steady and support the novice Ross.

“The general outline of these two islands could stand for the capes on this﻿—” He pulled a second map, this on transparent plastic, to fit over the first. The capes marked on the much larger body of land did slip over the modern islands with a surprising fit. The once large island, shattered and broken, could have produced the groups of atolls and islets they now prospected.

“How long﻿—” Karara mused aloud, “and why?”

===Chinese===
阿什耸了耸肩。他嘴角两旁绷出了深深的括弧纹，那是半年前还没有的。他动作生硬，不复当年那般从容流畅——那时候，他以不惊不乱的镇定面对时间旅行的浩渺距离，用一份自信扶持、支撑着新手罗斯。

「这两座岛的总体轮廓，可以对上这上面的海角——」他抽出第二张图，一张透明塑料图，覆在第一张上。标在那块大得多的陆地上的海角，恰好滑到现代这两座岛的位置上，吻合得令人惊讶。那座昔日的大岛，经天崩地裂、支离破碎之后，正好可能形成他们如今勘探的这群环礁与小岛。

「过了多久——」卡拉拉出声沉吟，「又是为了什么？」

===Original===
Ashe shrugged. “Ten thousand years, five, two.” He shook his head. “We have no idea. It’s apparent that there must have been some worldwide cataclysm here to change the contours of the land masses so much. We may have to wait on a return space flight to bring a ’copter or a hydroplane to explore farther.” His hand swept beyond the boundaries of the map to indicate the whole of Hawaika.

“A year, maybe two, before we could hope for that,” Ross cut in. “Then we’ll have to depend on whether the Council believes this important enough.” The contrariness which spiked his tongue whenever Karara was present made him say that without thinking. Then the twitch of Ashe’s lip brought home Ross’s error. Gordon needed reassurance now, not a recitation of the various ways their mission could be doomed.

“Look here!” Ross came to the table, his hand sweeping past Karara, as he used his forefinger for a pointer. “We know that what we want could be easily overlooked, even with the dolphins helping us to check. This whole area’s too big. And you know that it is certain that whatever might be down there would be hidden with sea growths. Suppose ten of us start out in a semicircle from about here and go as far as this point, heading inland. Video-cameras here and here﻿ ﻿… comb the whole sector inch by inch if we have to. After all, we have plenty of time and manpower.”

===Chinese===
阿什耸耸肩。「一万年，五千年，或者两千年。」他摇了摇头。「我们无从知晓。显然，这里必定发生过一场全球性的大灾变，才会把陆块的轮廓改变到这个地步。我们也许得等一趟返程的太空航班，运来一架直升机或水上飞机，才能往更远处探。」他的手一挥，扫出地图边界之外，指向整个哈瓦伊卡。

「少说一年，说不定两年，才指望得上。」罗斯插嘴，「到时候还得看理事会觉不觉得这事够重要。」每当卡拉拉在场，那股顶着来的别扭劲儿就会刺着他的舌尖，让他想都不想就说出这种话。随即阿什唇角一抽，罗斯才醒悟到自己错了。戈登现在需要的是宽慰，不是听人历数这趟任务可能完蛋的种种缘由。

「看这儿！」罗斯走到桌边，手臂从卡拉拉面前一扫而过，伸出一根食指当指示棒。「我们知道，要找的东西很容易被漏掉，哪怕有海豚帮着核查也一样。这一整片区域太大了。而且你们清楚，底下不管有什么，肯定都埋在海生植物底下。假如我们十个人从大概这儿出发，排成一个半圆，一直推进到这个点，朝内陆方向去。摄像机架在这儿、这儿……必要的话，把整个扇形区一寸一寸梳一遍。反正我们有的是时间和人力。」

===Original===
Karara laughed softly. “Manpower﻿—always manpower, Ross? But there is woman-power, too. And we have perhaps even sharper sight. But this is a good idea, Gordon. Let me see﻿—” she began to tell off names on her fingers, “PaKeeKee, Vaeoha, Hori, Liliha, Taema, Ui, Hono’ura﻿—they are the best in the water. Me﻿ ﻿… you, Gordon, Ross. That makes ten with keen eyes to look, and always there are Tino-rau and Taua. We will take supplies and camp here on this island which looks so much like a finger crooked to beckon. Yes, somehow that beckoning finger seems to me to promise better fortune. Shall we plan it so?”

Some of the tight look was gone from Ashe’s face, and Ross relaxed. This was what Gordon needed﻿—not to be sitting in here going over maps, reports, reworking over and over their scant leads. Ashe had always been a field man; and the settlement work had been stultifying, a laborious chore for him.

When Karara had gone Ross dropped down on the bunk against the side wall.

===Chinese===
卡拉拉轻轻笑了。「人力——张口闭口人力，罗斯？可还有女人力呢。没准我们的眼力还更尖些。不过这主意不错，戈登。让我想想——」她扳着手指头数起名字来，「帕基基（PaKeeKee）、瓦奥哈（Vaeoha）、霍里（Hori）、莉莉哈（Liliha）、塔埃马（Taema）、乌伊（Ui）、霍诺乌拉（Hono'ura）——她们水性最好。再加上我……你，戈登，还有罗斯。凑成十个眼尖的去找，外带随时待命的蒂诺-劳和塔乌亚。我们带上补给，到这座岛上扎营——它活像一根弯起来招人的手指。没错，也不知怎么，那根召唤的手指在我眼里就是预示着好运。就这么定，好不好？」

阿什脸上那种紧绷的神色褪去了几分，罗斯也松了口气。这才是戈登需要的——而不是闷在屋里翻地图、看报告，把那几条可怜的线索翻来覆去地嚼。阿什从来是跑野外的人；殖民地的案头事务早把他憋得没了生气，成了一件劳形苦役。

卡拉拉走后，罗斯一屁股跌坐在靠侧墙的铺位上。

===Original===
“What *did* happen here, do you think?” Half was real interest in the mystery they had mulled over and over since they had landed on a Hawaika which diverged so greatly from the maps; the other half, a desire to keep Ashe thinking on a subject removed from immediate worries. “An atomic war?”

“Could be. There are old radiation traces. But these aliens had, I’m sure, progressed beyond atomics. Suppose, just suppose, they could tamper with the weather, with the balance of the planet’s crust? We don’t know the extent of their powers, how they would use them. They had a colony here once, or there would have been no guide tape. And that is all we are sure of.”

“Suppose”﻿—Ross rolled over on his stomach, pillowed his head on his arms﻿—“we could uncover some of that knowledge﻿—”

===Chinese===
「你觉得这儿*到底*出过什么事？」这一半是真心好奇那个谜——自从降落在这个与地图大相径庭的哈瓦伊卡上，他们已把它翻来覆去琢磨了无数遍；另一半，是想让阿什的心思离开眼下的烦忧，落到别处去。「一场原子战争？」

「有可能。这里有古老的辐射痕迹。但我敢肯定，这些外星人早已越过了原子能的时代。假设一下，仅仅是假设——他们能干预天气，能动行星地壳的平衡呢？我们不知道他们的力量有多强，也不知道他们会怎么用。他们曾在这里有过一块殖民地，否则就不会留下那条导航磁带。而我们确凿知道的，也就这么多。」

「假设」——罗斯翻了个身趴着，把头枕在手臂上——「我们能挖出那类知识的一角呢——」

===Original===
The twitch was back at Ashe’s lips. “That’s the risk we have to run now.”

“Risk?”

“Would you give a child one of those hand weapons we found in the derelict?”

“Naturally not!” Ross snapped and then saw the point. “You mean﻿—*we* aren’t to be trusted?”

===Chinese===
阿什的唇角又抽动了一下。「这正是我们如今必须冒的险。」

「险？」

「你会把我们在那艘废船里找到的手持武器，递给一个小孩吗？」

「当然不会！」罗斯厉声回了一句，随即品出了话里的意思。「你是说——*我们*也不可信？」

===Original===
The answer was plain to read in Ashe’s expression.

“Then why this whole setup, this hunt for what might mean trouble?”

“The old pinch, the bad one. What if the Reds discover something first? They drew some planets in the tape lottery, remember. It’s a seesaw between us﻿—we advance here, they there. We have to keep up the race or lose it. They must be combing their stellar colonies for a few answers just as furiously as we are.”

“So, we go into the past to hunt if we have to. Well, I think I could do without answers such as the Baldies would know. But I will admit that I would like to know what did happen here﻿—two, five, ten thousand years ago.”

===Chinese===
答案明明白白写在阿什的表情里。

「那何必搞这一整套布置，何苦来找这些可能招来麻烦的东西？」

「老难题了，最头疼的那种。要是红方先发现了什么呢？别忘了，磁带抽签他们可是也分到了几颗行星的。我们之间就是一场跷跷板——我们在这儿推进，他们就在那儿推进。这场竞赛必须咬住，不然就输了。他们此刻肯定也在自己的星际殖民地里翻天覆地地找答案，跟我们一样凶。」

「所以啊，不得已就得钻进过去里找。哼，秃头人知道的那类答案，我倒觉得不要也罢。不过我承认，我确实想知道这里到底出过什么事——两千年前，五千年前，还是一万年前。」

===Original===
Ashe stood up and stretched. For the first time he smiled. “Do you know, I rather like the idea of fishing off Karara’s beckoning finger. Maybe she’s right about that changing our luck.”

Ross kept his face carefully expressionless as he got up to prepare their evening meal.

===Chinese===
阿什站起身，伸展了一下四肢。他头一回笑了。「你知道吗，去卡拉拉那根招福的手指边上钓鱼，这主意我相当喜欢。说不定她说得对，这能转转运。」

罗斯起身去准备晚饭，脸上小心翼翼地不留一丝表情。

## II Lair of Mano-Nui / II 马诺-努伊之巢

===Original===
Just under the surface of the water the sea was warm, weird life showed colors Ross could name, shades he could not. The corals, the animals masquerading as plants, the plants disguised as animals which inhabited the oceans of Terra, had their counterparts here. And the settlers had given them the familiar names, though the crabs, the fish, the anemones, and weeds of the shallow lagoons and reefs were not identical with Terran creatures. The trouble was that there was too much, such a wealth of life to attract the eyes, hold attention, that it was difficult to keep to the job at hand﻿—the search for what was not natural, for what had no normal place here.

As the land seduced the senses and bewitched the off-worlder, so did the sea have its enchantment to pull one from duty. Ross resolutely skimmed by a forest of weaving, waving lace which varied from a green which was almost black to a pale tint he could not truly identify. Among those waving fans lurked ghost-fish, finned swimmers transparent enough so that one could sight, through their pallid sides, the evidences of recently ingested meals.

The Terrans had begun their sweep-search a half hour ago, slipping overboard from a ferry canoe, heading in toward the checkpoint of the finger isle, forming an arc of expert divers, men and girls so at home in the ocean that they should be able to make the discovery Ashe needed﻿—if such did exist.

Mystery built upon mystery on Hawaika, Ross thought as he used his spear-gun to push aside a floating banner of weed in order to peer below its curtain. The native life of this world must always have been largely aquatic. The settlers had discovered only a few small animals on the islands. The largest of which was the burrower, a creature not unlike a miniature monkey in that it had hind legs on which it walked erect and forepaws, well clawed for digging purposes, which it used with as much skill and dexterity as a man used hands. Its body was hairless and it was able to assume, chameleon-like, the color of the soil and rocks where it denned. The head was set directly on its bowed shoulders without vestige of neck; and it had round bubbles of eyes near the top of its skull, a nose which was a single vertical slit, and a wide mouth fanged for crushing the shelled creatures on which it fed. All in all, to Terran eyes, it was a vaguely repulsive creature, but as far as the settlers had been able to discover it was the highest form of land life. The smaller rodentlike things, the two species of wingless diving birds, and an odd assortment of reptiles and amphibians sharing the island were all the burrowers’ prey.

===Chinese===
紧贴水皮之下，海水温暖，奇异的生灵展现出罗斯叫得出名的颜色，也有他叫不出的色度。珊瑚、伪装成植物的动物、假扮成动物的植物——这些栖息在特拉海洋里的成员，在此地各有其对应者。殖民者便给它们安上了熟悉的名字，尽管浅潟湖与礁盘上的蟹、鱼、海葵和海藻，与特兰的同类并不一模一样。麻烦在于东西太多了，如此丰盈的生命攫住目光、勾住心神，让人难以专心手头的正事——搜寻那些不自然的东西，那些在此地没有正常位置可归的东西。

陆地对感官的诱惑、对外来者的蛊惑有多大，大海便也有多大的魔力把人从职责上拽开。罗斯毅然掠过一片摇曳起伏的蕾丝之林，其色泽从近乎墨黑的浓绿，到一种他实在无法辨认的浅调，不一而足。那些飘摆的扇叶之间潜伏着幽灵鱼——这些长鳍的泳者透明得很，透过它们苍白的侧腹，能看清刚刚吃下的食物的痕迹。

特兰人半小时前便开始了扫掠式搜索，从一艘渡运独木舟上滑入水中，朝手指岛的检查点游进，排成一道由行家潜水员组成的弧线——这些小伙子和大姑娘在海洋里如在家中一般自在，理应能做出阿什需要的发现——倘若那东西当真存在的话。

哈瓦伊卡的谜上加谜，罗斯一边想，一边用鱼枪拨开一面漂浮的海藻幔，好窥看幔子底下的东西。这个世界的土产生命，想必向来以水栖为主。殖民者在岛上只发现过寥寥几种小动物。其中个头最大的是「掘土兽」——这家伙颇像一只缩小版的猴子：长着两条能直立行走的后腿，一对专为掘土而生的利爪前肢，用起来跟人使唤双手一样灵巧娴熟。它身子无毛，能像变色龙一样变出巢穴所在处泥土岩石的颜色。脑袋直接安在佝偻的肩膀上，连一点脖子的痕迹也没有；颅顶附近鼓着一对圆泡似的眼睛，鼻子只是一道竖缝，一张阔嘴生满獠牙，专门碾碎它赖以为生的带壳生物。总而言之，在特兰人眼里，这是个隐约招人嫌的家伙，但就殖民者目前所知，它已是陆地生命的最高形态。那些更小的鼠形小兽、两种不会飞的潜水鸟，还有同住一岛的各色爬虫与两栖动物，统统是掘土兽的口中食。

===Original===
A world of sea and islands, what type of native intelligent life had it once supported? Or had this been only a galactic colony, with no native population before the coming of the stellar explorers? Ross hovered above a dark pocket where the bottom had suddenly dipped into a saucer-shaped depression. The sea growth about the rim rippled in the water raggedly, but there was something about its general outline.﻿ ﻿…

Ross began a circumference of that hollow. Allowing for the distortion of the growths which had formed lumpy excrescences or reached turrets toward the surface﻿—yes, allowing for those﻿—this was decidedly something out of the ordinary! The depression was too regular, too even, Ross was certain of that. With a thrill of excitement he began a descent into the cup, striving to trace signs which would prove his suspicion correct.

How many years, centuries, had the slow coverage of the sea life gathered there, flourished, died, with other creatures to build anew on the remains? Now there was only a hint that the depression had other than a natural beginning.

===Chinese===
一个由海洋和岛屿组成的世界，曾经养育过什么样的土著智慧生命？抑或它只是一块银河殖民地，在星际探索者到来之前根本没有土著居民？罗斯悬浮在一个黑黢黢的凹坑上方——海底在那里骤然下陷，形成一个碟形洼坑。坑缘四周的海生植物在水中参差披拂，可那整体轮廓总有些说不出的名堂。……

罗斯开始沿着那洼坑的边绕圈。把那些长成疙瘩赘瘤、或朝水面伸出塔尖的海生植物的变形都算进去——对，就算把这些都打进去——这也分明是件异乎寻常的东西！这洼坑太规整、太匀称了，罗斯对此确信无疑。他怀着一线激动的战栗，开始朝碟坑里下潜，努力搜寻能证明自己猜想无误的迹象。

多少年、多少个世纪了，海底的生命缓缓地在那里聚积、繁盛、死去，又有别的生物在前辈的残骸上重新营建？如今只剩下一星暗示，表明这洼坑的起点并非天然。

===Original===
Anchoring with a one-handed grip on a spike of Hawaikan coral﻿—smoother than the Terran species﻿—Ross aimed the butt of his spear-gun at the nearest wall of the saucer, striving to reach into a crevice between two lumps of growth and so probe into what might lie behind. The spear rebounded; there was no breaking that crust with such a fragile tool. But perhaps he would have better luck lower down.

The depression was deeper than he had first judged. Now the light which existed in the shallows vanished. Red and yellow as colors went, but Ross was aware of blues and greens in shades and tints which were not visible above. He switched on his diving torch, and color returned within its beam. A swirl of weed, pink in the light, became darkly emerald beyond as if it possessed the chameleon ability of the burrowers.

He was distracted by that phenomenon, and so he transgressed the diver’s rule of never becoming so absorbed in surroundings as to forget caution. Just when did Ross become aware of that shadow below? Was it when a school of ghost-fish burst unexpectedly between weed growths, and he turned to follow them with the torch? Then the outer edge of his beam caught the movement of a shape, a flutter in the water of the gloomy depths.

===Chinese===
罗斯单手扣住一株哈瓦伊卡珊瑚的尖枝稳住身子——它比特拉的品种更光滑——随后将鱼枪托对准碟坑最近的坑壁，竭力伸进两团海生隆起之间的缝隙，探一探背后可能藏着什么。枪托弹了回来；用这样一件脆弱的家伙，是砸不破那层硬壳的。不过，往更低处去，也许运气会好些。

这洼坑比他起初判断的更深。此刻，浅水里尚存的光亮消失了。红色和黄色到此为止，但罗斯知道，这里还有种种在水面上看不见的蓝色与绿色的深浅调子。他拧亮潜水手电，光束之内色彩重临。一缕海藻在光中是粉色的，出了光束便转为幽深翡翠绿，仿佛也长了掘土兽那套变色的本事。

他看这景象看得分了神，于是触犯了潜水员的大忌——绝不能看周围看得入了迷，忘了警惕。罗斯到底是什么时候觉察到下方那团阴影的？是那一群幽灵鱼冷不丁从海藻丛间窜出、他转身用手电去追的时候吗？就在那时，光束的外缘捕到了一个形体的动静，幽暗深处的水里有什么东西一掠而过。

===Original===
Ross swung around, his back to the wall of the saucer, as he aimed the torch down at what was arising there. The light caught and held for a long moment of horror something which might have come out of the nightmares of his own world. Afterward Ross knew that the monster was not as large as it seemed in that endless minute of fear, perhaps no bigger than the dolphins.

He had had training in shark-infested seas on Terra, been carefully briefed against the danger from such hunters of the deep and ocean jungles. But this kind of thing had only existed before in the fairy tales of his race as the dragon of old lore. A scaled head with wide eyes gleaming in the light beam with cold and sullen hate, a gaping mouth fang-filled, a horn-set muzzle, that long, undulating neck and, below it, the half-seen bulk of a monstrous body.

His spear-gun, the knife at his waist belt, neither were protection against this! Yet to turn his back on that rising head was more than Ross could do. He pulled himself back against the wall of the saucer. The thing before him did not rush to attack. Plainly it had seen him and now it moved with the leisure of a hunter having no fears concerning the eventual outcome of the hunt. But the light appeared to puzzle it and Ross kept the beam shining straight into those evil eyes.

===Chinese===
罗斯猛地转身，背抵碟坑的坑壁，将手电对准底下正在上浮的东西。光束逮住了那东西，钉在那里，让人在漫长的一瞬里遍体生寒——它简直像是从他自己世界的梦魇里爬出来的。事后罗斯才明白，那怪物并没有他在那没有尽头的一分钟恐惧里显得那么大，说不定并不比海豚大多少。

他曾在特拉鲨群出没的海域受训，受过细致的告诫，提防深海与海洋丛林中这类猎手的危险。可这种东西，从前只在他的种族的童话里存在过，是古老传说中的龙。一颗覆鳞的头颅，一双在光束里闪着冰冷阴沉恨意的阔眼，一张獠牙密布的血盆大口，一只生角的口鼻，还有那长长的、波浪般起伏的脖颈，以及脖颈之下、隐约可见的一副庞大身躯。

他的鱼枪、腰带上那把刀，在这家伙面前都算不上护身之物！可要他对着那颗冉冉上升的头转过背去，罗斯又做不到。他把自己往碟坑壁上又贴了贴。面前的东西并没有扑上来进攻。显然它已经看见了他，此刻的举动从容不迫，一副猎手做派——对这场狩猎的最终结局毫不担心。只是那道光似乎让它犯惑，罗斯便死死把光束直直照进那双凶眼里。

===Original===
The shock of the encounter was wearing off; now Ross edged his flipper into a crevice to hold him steady while his hand went to the sonic-com at his waist. He tapped out a distress call which the dolphins could relay to the swimmers. The swaying dragon head paused, held rigid on a stiff, scaled column in the center of the saucer. That sonic vibration either surprised or bothered the hunter, made it wary.

Ross tapped again. The belief that if he tried to escape, he was lost, that only while he faced it so had he any chance, grew stronger. The head was only inches below the level of his flippered feet as he held to the weeds.

Again that weaving movement, the rise of head, a tremor along the serpent neck, an agitation in the depths. The dragon was on the move again. Ross aimed the light directly at the head. The scales, as far as he could determine, were not horny plates but lapped, silvery ovals such as a fish possessed. And the underparts of the monster might even be vulnerable to his spear. But knowing the way a Terran shark could absorb the darts of that weapon and survive, Ross feared to attack except as a last resort.

===Chinese===
遭遇的震骇渐渐退去；罗斯把一只脚蹼卡进岩缝稳住身体，手则摸向腰间的声波通话器。他敲出一组遇险信号，海豚可以转发给水上的同伴。那颗摇曳的龙头顿住了，僵在碟坑中央一根僵直的鳞柱上。那阵声波震动不是惊着了就是搅扰了这位猎手，让它起了戒心。

罗斯又敲了一遍。他越来越笃信：这时候若想逃，就死定了；只有像这样正对着它，才有一线生机。他抓着海草，那颗头离他脚蹼所在的高度只有几英寸。

又是那般摇摆的动作，头颅抬升，蛇颈上一阵颤动，深处一阵骚乱。龙又动了。罗斯把手电直直对准那颗头。据他辨认，那些鳞片并非角质硬甲，而是一片搭一片的银色椭圆，跟鱼的鳞一样。而这怪物的腹面，说不定恰恰经不起他这一枪。只是他深知特拉的鲨鱼能带着那武器的倒刺照样活命，除非万不得已，罗斯不敢出手。

===Original===
Above and to his left there was a small hollow where in the past some portion of the growths had been ripped away. If he could fit himself into that crevice, perhaps he could keep the dragon at bay until help arrived. Ross moved with all the skill he had. His hand closed upon the edge of the niche and he whirled himself up, just making it into that refuge as the head lashed at him wickedly. His suspicion that the dragon would attack anything on the run was well founded, and he knew he had no hope of winning to the surface above.

Now he stood in the crevice, facing outward, watching the head darting in the water. He had switched off the torch, and the loss of light appeared to bewilder the reptile for some precious seconds. Ross pulled as far back into the niche as he could, until the point of one shoulder touched a surface which was sleek, smooth, and cold. The shock of that contact almost sent him hurtling out again.

Gripping the spear before him in his right hand, Ross cautiously felt behind him with the left. His finger tips glided over a seamless surface where the growths had been torn or peeled away. Though he could not, or dared not, turn his head to see, he was certain that this was his proof that the walls of the saucer had been fashioned and placed there by some intelligent creature.

===Chinese===
他左上方有个小凹洞，早先有一部分海生植物从那里被撕掉了。若能把自己塞进那条缝隙，也许就能把龙挡在外面，撑到救兵赶来。罗斯拿出全身本事挪过去。他的手一扣住洞缘，整个人便旋身翻上去——堪堪在龙头恶狠狠抽打过来的一瞬挤进了那处避难所。他先前猜测龙见着逃跑的东西就会攻击，如今证实猜得不错；他也明白，自己绝无希望抢到上方的海面去。

此刻他立在缝隙里，脸朝外，盯着那颗头在水里东突西窜。他已关掉手电，光亮一失，那爬虫似乎懵了好几秒——宝贵的几秒。罗斯尽量往洞深处缩，直到一侧肩头碰到一个滑腻、光洁而冰冷的表面。那一触的惊骇，差点把他整个人又撞出去。

右手把鱼枪握在身前，罗斯用左手小心翼翼地向身后摸索。指尖滑过一个浑然无缝的表面，那里的海生植物是被撕掉或剥掉的。他没法——或者不敢——回头去看，但他确信，这就是他的证据：这碟坑的壁，是某种智慧生物造好、安放到这里来的。

===Original===
The dragon had risen, hovering now in the water directly before the entrance to Ross’s hole, its neck curled back against its bulk. It had wide flippers moving like planes to hold it poised. The body, sloping from a massive round of shoulders to a tapering rear, was vaguely familiar. If one provided a Terran seal with a gorgon head and scales in place of fur, the effect would be similar. But Ross was assuredly not facing a seal at this moment.

Slight movement of the flippers kept it as stabilized as if it sprawled on a supporting surface. With the neck flattened against the body, the head curved downward until the horn on its snout pointed the tip straight at Ross’s middle. The Terran steadied his spear-gun. The dragon’s eyes were its most vulnerable targets; if the creature launched the attack, Ross would aim for them.

Both man and dragon were so intent upon their duel that neither was conscious of the sudden swirl overhead. A sleek dark shape struck down, skimming across the humped-back ridge of the dragon. Some of the settlers had empathy with the dolphins to a high degree, but Ross’s own powers of contact were relatively feeble.

===Chinese===
龙已经升了上来，就悬在罗斯那个洞口正前方的水里，脖颈蜷收回躯干上。它宽大的鳍肢像机翼一般摆动着，把自己稳稳悬住。那躯体，从浑圆厚重的一副肩部斜削向收细的尾部，看着隐约眼熟。倘若给一头特拉海豹装上蛇发妖的头、拿鳞片换掉皮毛，效果大致便是如此。只是此刻罗斯面对的，铁定不是一头海豹。

鳍肢轻轻拨动，让它稳得如同趴在一面托底上。脖颈贴平在身上，头颅向下弯，直到口鼻上的那只角尖直指罗斯的中段。特兰人端稳了鱼枪。龙的眼睛是它最不禁打的靶子；这家伙一旦发起攻击，罗斯就冲那儿瞄准。

人和龙都在全神贯注地盯着这场对峙，谁都没留意头顶骤然卷起的水涡。一道光滑的黑色身影俯冲而下，贴着龙那隆起的背脊掠过。殖民者中有人与海豚共感极深，罗斯自己的感应能力却相对微弱。

===Original===
Only now he was given an assurance of aid, and a suggestion to attack. The dragon head writhed, twisted as the reptile attempted to see above and behind its own length. But the dolphin was only a streak fast disappearing. And that writhing changed the balance the monster had maintained, pushing it toward Ross.

The Terran fired too soon and without proper aim, so the dart snaked past the head. But the harpoon line half hooked about the neck and seemed to confuse the creature. Ross squirmed as far back as he could into his refuge and drew his knife. Against those fangs the weapon was an almost useless toy, but it was all he had.

Again the dolphin dived in attack on the reptile, this time seizing in its mouth the floating cord of the harpoon and giving it a jerk which jolted the dragon even more off balance, pulling it away from Ross’s niche and out into the center of the saucer.

===Chinese===
但此刻他得到了一个担保——援手来了，还有一个暗示——进攻。龙头一阵扭动翻转，那爬虫竭力想看清自己身长上方和身后的东西。可海豚已只剩一道飞快远去的影。而那一阵扭动打破了怪物原本保持的平衡，把它朝罗斯这边推了过来。

特兰人出手太急，瞄得也不正，倒刺从头侧擦了过去。可鱼叉的索线却半缠上了那截脖颈，似乎搅乱了这家伙的心神。罗斯拼命往避难所深处缩，抽出刀来。对着那口獠牙，这件家伙什近乎无用的玩具，可他手里也只有它了。

海豚再次俯冲攻向那爬虫，这回一口咬住漂着的鱼叉索，猛地一拽，把龙晃得愈发失了平衡，拖离罗斯的洞口，拽到碟坑正中央去。

===Original===
There were two dolphins in action now, Ross saw, playing the dragon as matadors might play a bull, keeping the creature disturbed by their agile maneuvers. Whatever prey came naturally to the Hawaikan monster was not of this type, and the creature was not prepared to deal effectively with their teasing, dodging tactics. Neither had touched the beast, but they kept it constantly striving to get at them.

Though it swam in circles attempting to face its teasers, the dragon did not abandon the level before Ross’s refuge, and now and then it darted its head at him, unwilling to give up its prey. Only one of the dolphins frisked and dodged above now as the sonic on Ross’s belt vibrated against his lower ribs with its message warning to be prepared for further action. Somewhere above, his own kind gathered. Hurriedly he tapped out in code his warning in return.

Two dolphins busy again, their last dive over the dragon pushing the monster down past Ross’s niche toward the saucer’s depths. Then they flashed up and away. The dragon was rising in turn, but coming to meet the Hawaikan creature was a ball giving off light, bringing sharp vision and color with it.

===Chinese===
现在上阵的是两只海豚了，罗斯看得清楚——它们斗这条龙，如同斗牛士戏弄公牛，用灵巧的腾挪让这家伙不得安生。哈瓦伊卡这怪物天生捕食的猎物里，显然没有这一路货色，它一时拿不出有效的手段来对付这种挑逗闪避的战术。两只海豚谁都没碰着那畜生，却始终勾着它穷追不舍。

龙尽管绕着圈游、想转过身来面对戏弄它的对手，却不肯放弃罗斯避难所前方的这一层水域，时不时朝他猛啄一口，不甘心就这么丢了到嘴的猎物。头顶上如今只剩一只海豚在腾挪闪避，而罗斯腰间的声波通话器抵着下肋震了起来，传来讯息：准备接应后续行动。上方某处，他的同类正在聚集。他急忙用代码敲出回敬的警告。

两只海豚又忙开了，它们最后一次从龙身上方俯冲，把这怪物压过罗斯的洞口、逼向碟坑深处。随后它们一闪而上，远去了。龙跟着又往上升，可迎着这哈瓦伊卡怪物的，是一枚发着光的圆球，把清晰的视界和色彩一并带了下来。

===Original===
Ross’s arm swung up to shield his eyes. There was a flash; such answering vibration carried through the waves that even his nerves, far less sensitive than those of the life about him, reacted. He blinked behind his mask. A fish floated by, spiraling up, its belly exposed. And about him growths drooped, trailed lifelessly through the water; while there was a now motionless bulk sinking to the obscurity of the depression floor. A weapon perfected on Terra to use against sharks and barracuda had worked here to kill what could have been more formidable prey.

The Terran wriggled out of the niche, rose to meet another swimmer. As Ashe descended, Ross relayed his news via the sonic. The dolphins were already nosing into the depths in pursuit of their late enemy.

“Look here﻿—” Ross guided Ashe to the crevice which had saved him, aimed the torch beam into it. He had been right! There was a long groove in the covering built up by the growths; a vertical strip some six feet long, of a uniform gray, showed. Ashe touched the find and then gave the alert via the sonic code.

===Chinese===
罗斯扬臂护住眼睛。一道闪光；随之而来的震动穿透水波，连他这副远不如周围生灵敏锐的神经都有了反应。他在面罩后眨了眨眼。一条鱼肚皮翻白，打着旋浮了上去。四周的植物蔫头耷脑，死气沉沉地拖在水里；而那一大团一动不动的躯壳，正沉向洼坑底部的一片幽暗。一件在特拉为对付鲨鱼和梭鱼而完善起来的武器，在这里照样管用，杀死了一头本可能更难对付的猎物。

特兰人从缝隙里挣出来，上浮去迎接另一个游泳的人。阿什下潜过来时，罗斯通过声波通话器把消息传了过去。海豚们早已一头扎进深处，追它们方才的仇敌去了。

「看这儿——」罗斯把阿什引到那条救过他命的缝隙前，把手电光束照进去。他没猜错！海生植物层层结起的覆盖物上有一道长槽，露出一条约六英尺长、通体灰色的竖直带面。阿什碰了碰那处发现，随即用声波代码发出警报讯号。

===Original===
“Metal or an alloy, we’ve found it!”

But what did they have? Even after an hour’s exploration by the full company, Ashe’s expert search with his knowledge of artifacts and ancient remains, they were still baffled. It would require labor and tools they did not have, to clear the whole of the saucer. They could be sure only of its size and shape, and the fact that its walls were of an unknown substance which the sea could cloak but not erode. For the length of gray surface showed not the slightest pitting or time wear.

Down at its centermost point they found the dragon’s den, an arch coated with growth, before which sprawled the body of the creature. That was dragged aloft with the dolphins’ aid, to be taken ashore for study. But the arch itself﻿ ﻿… was that part of some old installation?

===Chinese===
「金属，要么是合金——我们找到了！」

可他们找到的到底是什么？即便全队人马勘查了一个钟头，即便有阿什凭他对器物与古遗存的知识行家般地搜寻，他们依旧一头雾水。要清空整个碟坑，需要他们并不具备的人力和工具。他们能有把握的，只有它的大小和形状，以及一个事实：坑壁由一种未知的物质构成，海洋能把它掩住，却磨不蚀它。因为那段露出灰色的表面，不见一丝麻点，不见一丝岁月的磨耗。

在碟坑最中心的底点上，他们找到了龙的巢穴——一道覆满海生植物的拱门，那具尸体就瘫在门前。靠着海豚帮忙，尸体被拖了上去，要运上岸研究。可那道拱门本身……它是某座古老设施的一部分吗？

===Original===
Torches to the fore, they entered its shadow, only to remain baffled. Here and there were patches of the same gray showing in its interior. Ashe dug the butt of his spear-gun into the sand on the flooring to uncover another oval depression. But what it all signified or what had been its purpose, they could not guess.

“Set up the peep-probe here?” Ross asked.

Ashe’s head moved in a slow negative. “Look farther﻿ ﻿… spread out,” the sonic clicked.

===Chinese===
手电打前，他们游进拱门的阴影里，结果依旧满腹疑团。内部这里一块那里一块，露出同样的灰色。阿什用鱼枪托刨开地面上的泥沙，又清出一片椭圆形的凹坑。可这一切意味着什么、当初派何用场，他们猜不出来。

「把窥视探头架在这儿？」罗斯问。

阿什的脑袋缓缓摇了摇。「再往远找……散开些。」声波通话器嗒嗒作响。

===Original===
Within a matter of minutes the dolphins reported new remains﻿—two more saucers, each larger than the first, set in a line on the ocean floor, pointing directly to Karara’s Finger Island. Cautiously explored, these were discovered to be free of any but harmless life; they stirred up no more dragons.

When the Terrans came ashore on Finger Island to rest and eat their midday meal one of the men paced along the beached dragon. Ashore it lost none of its frightening aspect. And seeing it, even beached and dead, Ross wondered at his luck in surviving the encounter without a scratch.

“I think that this one would be alone,” PaKeeKee commented. “Where there is an eater of this size, there is usually only one.”

===Chinese===
没过几分钟，海豚便报告发现了新的遗迹——又是两座碟坑，一座比一座大，在海底排成一线，直指卡拉拉的手指岛。小心探过之后，发现里面除了无害的生物空无一物；再没有惊出别的龙来。

特兰人上岸到手指岛上休整、吃午饭时，一名队员沿着那条被拖上滩的龙来回踱步。上了岸，它那骇人的架势分毫未减。看着它——哪怕已搁浅死去——罗斯都暗自惊叹自己的运气：那场遭遇，他竟毫发无伤地熬了过来。

「我看这一条是独居的。」帕基基评论道，「凡是有这种块头的食客的地方，通常只有一条。」

===Original===
“Mano-Nui!” The girl Taema shivered as she gave to this monster the name of the shark demon of her people. “Such a one is truly king shark in these waters! But why have we not sighted its like before? Tino-rau, Taua﻿ ﻿… they have not reported such﻿—”

“Probably because, as PaKeeKee says, these things are rare,” Ashe returned. “A carnivore of size would have to have a fairly wide hunting range, yet there’s evidence that this thing has laired in that den for some time. Which means that it must have a defined hunting territory allowing no trespassing from others of its species.”

Karara nodded. “Also it may hunt only at intervals, eat heavily, and lie quiet until that meal is digested. There are large snakes on Terra that follow that pattern. Ross was in its front yard when it came after him﻿—”

===Chinese===
「马诺-努伊！」姑娘塔埃马打了个寒噤，把族人鲨魔的名字安在了这怪物头上。「这样的家伙，当真是这片水域的鲨王！可我们以前怎么从没见过它的同类？蒂诺-劳、塔乌亚……它们也没报告过这种东西——」

「多半是因为，就像帕基基说的，这东西稀罕。」阿什回答，「这么大块的食肉动物，得有相当大的猎场才养得活。可有迹象表明，这家伙在那巢里盘踞了有些时日了。这就是说，它必定划定了自己的猎场，容不得同类越界闯入。」

卡拉拉点点头。「再说，它兴许隔三差五才猎一回食，一顿吃个够，然后趴着不动，等那顿饭消化完。特拉有些大蛇就是这个路数。罗斯当时正好闯进它家门口，它才追出来的——」

===Original===
“From now on”﻿—Ashe swallowed a quarter of fruit﻿—“we know what to watch for, and the weapon which will finish it off. Don’t forget that!”

The delicate mechanisms of their sonics had already registered the vibrations which would warn of a dragon’s presence, and the depth globes would then do the rest.

“Big skull, oversize for the body.” PaKeeKee squatted on his heels by the head lying on the sand at the end of the now fully extended neck.

===Chinese===
「从今往后」——阿什咽下一角水果——「我们知道该提防什么，也知道了能结果它的武器。别忘了这一条！」

他们声波通话器里的精密机件已经录下了那种震动，足以预警龙的临近，剩下的事，就交给深水弹好了。

「脑壳真大，跟身子比大得离谱。」帕基基蹲在头颅旁边，那颗头搁在沙地上，连着如今完全伸展开的脖颈末端。

===Original===
Ross had heretofore been more aware of the armament of that head, the fangs set in the powerful jaws, the horn on the snout. But PaKeeKee’s comment drew his attention to the fact that the scale-covered skull did dome up above the eye pits in a way to suggest ample brain room. Had the thing been intelligent? Karara put that into words:

“Rule One?” She went over to survey the carcass.

Ross resented her half question, whether it was addressed to him or mere thinking aloud on her part.

===Chinese===
在此之前，罗斯更留意的是那颗头的武装——结实颌骨里的獠牙、口鼻上的角。可帕基基一句话提醒了他：那覆鳞的脑壳，的确在眼窝上方隆起成穹，瞧着脑容量不小。这家伙有过智慧吗？卡拉拉把这疑问说出了口：

「第一守则？」她走过去打量那具尸体。

罗斯对她这半截问话很不是滋味，不管那是冲他来的，还是她自言自语。

===Original===
Rule One: Conserve native life to the fullest extent. Humanoid form may not be the only evidence of intelligence.

There were the dolphins to prove that point right on Terra. But did Rule One mean that you had to let a monster nibble at you because it might just be a high type of alien intelligence? Let Karara spout Rule One while backed into a crevice under water with that horn stabbing at her midsection!

“Rule One does not mean to forego self-defense,” Ashe commented mildly. “This thing is a hunter, and you can’t stop to apply recognition techniques when you are being regarded as legitimate prey. If you are the stronger, or an equal, yes﻿—stop and think before becoming aggressive. But in a situation like this﻿—take no chances.”

“Anyway, from now on,” Karara pointed out, “it could be possible to shock instead of kill.”

===Chinese===
第一守则：尽最大可能保全土著生命。人形未必是智慧的唯一凭证。

光在特拉，海豚就是这条守则的铁证。可第一守则难道意味着，就因为一头怪物没准是外星智慧的高级形态，你就得由着它啃你？让卡拉拉缩在水下岩缝里、顶着那只直戳她中段的角，再来讲她的第一守则吧！

「第一守则不是让人放弃自卫。」阿什温和地说，「这家伙是猎手，当人家把你当成正经猎物的时候，你可停不下来搞什么识别鉴定。你若更强，或旗鼓相当——对，那可以先停一停、想一想再动手。可眼下这种局面——千万别赌运气。」

「反正，从今往后，」卡拉拉指出，「咱们可以用震晕代替杀戮了。」

===Original===
“Gordon”﻿—PaKeeKee swung around﻿—“what have we found here﻿—besides this thing?”

“I can’t even guess. Except that those depressions were made for a purpose and have been there for a long time. Whether they were originally in the water, or the land sank, that we don’t know either. But now we have a site to set up the peep-probe.”

“We do that right away?” Ross wanted to know. Impatience bit at him. But Ashe still had a trace of frown. He shook his head.

“Have to make sure of our site, very sure. I don’t want to start any chain reaction on the other side of the time wall.”

===Chinese===
「戈登」——帕基基转过身来——「咱们在这儿发现的到底是什么——除了这家伙之外？」

「我连猜都猜不出。只知道那几处洼坑是带着目的造出来的，而且在那儿待了很久。它们起初就在水下，还是后来陆地沉了，这我们也不知道。不过现在，我们总算有了架设窥视探头的场址。」

「马上就干？」罗斯想知道。焦躁咬着他。可阿什眉间仍锁着一丝皱痕。他摇了摇头。

「场址必须敲死，敲得死死的。我不想在时间之墙的那一头，引爆什么连锁反应。」

===Original===
And he was right, Ross was forced to admit, remembering what had happened when the galactics had discovered the Red time gates and traced them forward to their twentieth-century source, ruthlessly destroying each station. The original colonists of Hawaika had been as giants to Terran pygmies when it came to technical knowledge. To use even a peep-probe indiscreetly near one of their outposts might bring swift and terrible retribution.

===Chinese===
他是对的，罗斯不得不承认——他想起当年银河人发现红方的时间之门、顺藤摸到它们二十世纪的源头、把一座座站台无情摧毁的事。论技术知识，哈瓦伊卡最初的殖民者之于特兰人，犹如巨人之于侏儒。哪怕只是在他们某处前哨的附近轻率地启用一台窥视探头，都可能招来迅疾而可怕的报应。

## III The Ancient Mariners / III 古代航海者

===Original===
Another map spread out and this time pinned down with small stones on beach gravel.

“Here, here, and here﻿—” Ashe’s finger indicated the points marked in a pattern which flared out from three sides of Finger Island. Each marked a set of three undersea depressions in perfect alliance with the land which, according to the galactic map, had once been a cape on a much larger land mass. Though the Terrans had found the ruins, if those saucers in the sea could be so termed, the remains had no meaning for the explorers.

“Do we set up here?” Ross asked. “If we could just get a report to send back.﻿ ﻿…” That might mean the difference between awakening the cooperation of the Project policy makers so that a flood of supplies and personnel would begin to head their way.

“We set up here,” Ashe decided.

He had selected a point between two of the lines where a reef would provide them with a secure base. And once that decision was made, the Terrans went into action.

Two days to go, to install the peep-probe and take some shots before the ship had to clear with or without their evidence. Together Ross and Ashe floated the installation out to the reef, Ui and Karara helping to tow the equipment and parts, the dolphins lending pushing noses on occasion. The aquatic mammals were as interested as the human beings they aided. And in water their help was invaluable. Had dolphins developed hands, Ross wondered fleetingly, would they have long ago wrested control of their native world﻿—or at least of its seas﻿—from the human kind?

===Chinese===
又一张地图摊了开来，这回用小石子压在滩石上。

「这儿，这儿，还有这儿——」阿什的手指点着图上一处处标记，从手指岛的三面呈扇形散开。每一点都标着一组海底洼坑，共三组，与那片陆地严丝合缝地对应着——按银河地图所示，那里曾是块大得多的陆地上的一处海角。特兰人虽然找到了这些遗迹——若海里那些碟坑也配得上这个词的话——但这些残骸对探索者而言毫无意义可言。

「我们架在这儿？」罗斯问。「要是能弄出一份报告发回去就好。……」那可能就是关键的一步之差——足以唤醒「计划」决策者们的合作，让补给和人员如潮水般朝这里涌来。

「就架在这儿。」阿什拍板。

他选定的是两条线之间的一点，那里有一片礁盘，可为他们提供稳固的基地。决定一下，特兰人立刻行动起来。

还剩两天，要在飞船带着或不带着他们的证据离开之前，把窥视探头安装好、拍下一些画面。罗斯和阿什一起把装置浮运到礁盘，乌伊和卡拉拉帮着拖设备和部件，海豚们不时用鼻尖推一把。这些水栖哺乳动物跟它们帮助的人类一样兴致勃勃。在水里，它们的助力无可估量。海豚若是长出了手，会不会早就从人类手里夺过它们原生世界——至少是那片海洋——的控制权？罗斯脑中闪过这样一个念头。

===Original===
All the human beings worked with practiced ease, even while masked and submerged, to set the probe in place, aiming it landward at the check point of the Finger’s protruding nail of rock. After Ashe made the final adjustments, tested each and every part of the assembly, he gestured them in.

Karara’s swift hand movement asked a question, and Ashe’s sonic code-clicked in reply: “At twilight.”

Yes, dusk was the proper time for using a peep-probe. To see without risk of being sighted in return was their safeguard. Here Ashe had no historical data to guide him. Their search for the former inhabitants might be a long drawn-out process skipping across centuries as the machine was adjusted to Terran time eras.

“When were they here?” Back on shore Karara shook out her hair, spread it over her shoulders to dry. “How many hundred years back will the probe return?”

===Chinese===
所有人戴着面罩、没在水下，仍以娴熟自如的动作把探头安放到位，镜头朝向陆地，对准手指岛那截突出的指甲状岩石上的检查点。阿什做完最后的调校，把整套装置的每个部件逐一测试过，才朝他们一挥手，示意上岸。

卡拉拉敏捷地打了个手势发问，阿什的声波通话器嗒嗒地敲出回答：「黄昏时分。」

不错，黄昏正是使用窥视探头的恰当时刻。只看不被看，就是他们的护身符。在这里，阿什没有史料可以依凭。他们对昔日居民的搜寻，可能会是一场旷日持久的拉锯——随着机器对准一个个特拉式的时代，一跳一跳地跨过几百几百年。

「他们是什么时候在这儿的？」回到岸上，卡拉拉抖开头发，披在肩头晾干。「探头要回溯多少个百年？」

===Original===
“More likely thousands,” Ross commented. “Where will you start, Gordon?”

Ashe brushed sand from the page of the notebook he had steadied against one bent knee and gazed out at the reef where they had set the probe.

“Ten thousand years﻿—”

“Why?” Karara wanted to know. “Why that exact figure?”

===Chinese===
「更可能是几千年。」罗斯说。「你打算从哪儿入手，戈登？」

阿什拂去笔记本页上的沙粒——那本子架在他弓起的膝盖上——目光投向他们安放探头的礁盘。

「一万年前——」

「为什么？」卡拉拉想知道。「为什么偏偏是这个数？」

===Original===
“We know that galactic ships crashed on Terra then. So their commerce and empire﻿—if it was an empire﻿—was far-flung at that time. Perhaps they were at the zenith of their civilization; perhaps they were already on the down slope. I do not think they were near the beginning. So that date is as good a starting place as any. If we don’t hit what we’re after, then we can move forward until we do.”

“Do you think that there ever was a native population here?”

“Might have been.”

“But without any large land animals, no modern traces of any,” she protested.

“Of people?” Ashe shrugged. “Good answers for both. Suppose there was a worldwide epidemic of proportions to wipe out a species. Or a war in which they used forces beyond our comprehension to alter the whole face of this planet, which did happen﻿—the alteration, I mean. Several things could have removed intelligent life. Then such species as the burrowers could have developed or evolved from smaller, more primitive types.”

===Chinese===
「我们知道，银河飞船正是在那个年代坠毁在特拉的。可见他们的商路和帝国——如果那算个帝国的话——当时已伸得很远。也许他们正处在文明的顶峰；也许已在走下坡路。我不认为他们那时才刚起步。所以这个年代是个不坏的开局。要是没撞上我们要找的，就往前挪，直到撞上为止。」

「你觉得这里曾经有过土著居民吗？」

「兴许有过。」

「可这里没有大型陆生动物，连一点现代的踪迹都没有。」她提出异议。

「人算吗？」阿什耸耸肩。「两种情况都说得通。假设一场全球性的大瘟疫，烈到灭绝整个种族。或者一场战争，动用了我们无法理解的力量，改换了这颗行星的整张面孔——这倒确有其事，我是说地貌之变。好几种情况都可能铲除智慧生命。之后，掘土兽这类物种，完全可能由更小、更原始的形态发展演化而来。」

===Original===
“Those ape-things we found on the desert planet.” Ross thought back to their first voyage on the homing derelict. “Maybe they had once been men and were degenerating. And the winged people, they could have been less than men on their way up﻿—”

“Ape-things﻿ ﻿… winged people?” Karara interrupted. “Tell me!”

There was something imperious in her demand, but Ross found himself describing in detail their past adventures, first on the world of sand and sealed structures where the derelict had rested for a purpose its involuntary passengers had never understood, and then of the Terrans’ limited exploration of that other planet which might have been the capital world of a far-flung stellar empire. There they had made a pact with a winged people living in the huge buildings of a jungle-choked city.

“But you see”﻿—the Polynesian girl turned to Ashe when Ross had finished﻿—“you did find them﻿—these ape-things and the winged people. But here there are only the dragons and the burrowers. Are they the start or the finish? I want to know﻿—”

“Why?” Ashe asked.

===Chinese===
「我们在沙漠星球上发现的那些猿人。」罗斯回想起他们在那艘自动返航的废船上的首航。「也许他们曾经是人，正在退化。还有那些有翼人，他们或许尚未成人，正在向上走——」

「猿人……有翼人？」卡拉拉打断了他。「讲给我听！」

她的要求带着一股不容分说的劲儿，罗斯却发现自己娓娓讲起了往日的冒险——先是在那个遍布黄沙与封闭建筑的世界，废船曾在那里停驻，缘由如何，它那群不情愿的乘客始终没能明白；接着讲特兰人对另一颗行星的有限探索，那也许是个疆域辽阔的恒星帝国的都城之星。在那儿，他们与一个栖居于丛林湮没的巨城高楼中的有翼民族结过盟。

「可你们看——」罗斯讲完，波利尼西亚姑娘转向阿什——「你们确实找到了他们——那些猿人和有翼人。可这里只有龙和掘土兽。他们是起点还是终点？我想知道——」

「为什么想知道？」阿什问。

===Original===
“Not just because I am curious, though I am that also, but because we, too, must have a beginning and an end. Did we come up from the seas, rise to know and feel and think, just to return to such beginning at our end? If your winged people were climbing and your ape-things descending”﻿—she shook her head﻿—“it would be frightening to hold a cord of life, both ends in your hands. Is it good for us to see such things, Gordon?”

“Men have asked that question all their thinking lives, Karara. There have been those who have said no, who have turned aside and tried to halt the growth of knowledge here or there, attempted to make men stand still on one tread of a stairway. Only there is that in us which will not stop, ill-fitted as we may be for the climbing. Perhaps we shall be safe and untroubled here on Hawaika if I do not go out to that reef tonight. By that action I may bring real danger down on all of us. Yet I can not hold back for that. Could you?”

“No, I do not believe that I could,” she agreed.

“We are here because we are of those who must know﻿—volunteers. And being of that temperament, it is in us always to take the next step.”

“Even if it leads to a fall,” she added in a low tone.

===Chinese===
「不光是出于好奇——虽然好奇也是有的——更因为我们也必有开头和结局。我们从海里上来，站起来去认知、去感受、去思考，难道只为到头来回到那个起点吗？倘若你们的有翼人正在向上爬，猿人正在往下坠——」她摇了摇头——「那就像手里攥着生命之绳的两头，太可怕了。让我们看见这些东西，是好事吗，戈登？」

「人只要会思考，一辈子都在问这个问题，卡拉拉。历来也有人说不，有人掉头而去，想在这样那样的地方堵住知识的生长，想让整个人类在楼梯的某一级上站定不动。只是我们体内有那么一种东西，怎么也停不下来——哪怕我们未必适合攀高。如果我今晚不出那片礁盘，我们在哈瓦伊卡也许就平安无事。我这一去，也许会给大伙儿招来实实在在的危险。可我不能为此却步。你能吗？」

「不能，我想我做不到。」她表示同意。

「我们在这里，正因为我们属于非知道不可的那类人——志愿者。既然是这种脾性，我们心里永远会想迈出下一步。」

「哪怕下一步通向跌落。」她低声补了一句。

===Original===
Ashe gazed at her, though her own eyes were on the sea where a lace of waves marked the reef. Her words were ordinary enough, but Ross straightened to match Ashe’s stare. Why had he felt that odd instant of uneasiness as if his heart had fluttered instead of beating true?

“I know of you Time Agents,” Karara continued. “There were plenty of stories about you told while we were in training.”

“Tall tales, I can imagine, most of them.” Ashe laughed, but his amusement sounded forced to Ross.

“Perhaps. Though I do not believe that many could be any taller than the truth. And so also I have heard of that strict rule you follow, that you must do nothing which might alter the course of history. But suppose, suppose here that the course of history could be altered, that whatever catastrophe occurred might be averted? If that was done, what would happen to our settlement in the here and now?”

“I don’t know. That is an experiment which we have never dared to try, which we won’t try﻿—”

===Chinese===
阿什凝视着她，她自己的目光却落在海上，那里一线浪花勾出礁盘的轮廓。她的话本平淡无奇，罗斯却直起身来，与阿什的目光一齐落在她身上。他为何感到那一瞬古怪的不安，仿佛心口漏跳了一拍，而不是踏实有力地跳着？

「你们这些时间特工的事，我听说过。」卡拉拉接着说，「我们受训的时候，流传着关于你们的数不清的故事。」

「天花乱坠的故事吧，我猜大半都是。」阿什笑了，可在罗斯听来，那份笑意有些勉强。

「也许是。不过我不信有几个能吹得比真事更高。我也听说过你们恪守的那条铁律：不得做出任何可能改动历史进程的事。可是假设——就假设这里的历史进程能被改动，那场无论什么灾祸本可以避免呢？真要那样做了，我们此时此地的殖民点会怎么样？」

「我不知道。那是一场我们从来不敢做的实验，今后也不会去做——」

===Original===
“Not even if it would mean a chance of life for a whole native race?” she persisted.

“Alternate worlds then, maybe.” Ross’s imagination caught up that idea. “Two worlds from a change point in history,” he elaborated, noting her look of puzzlement. “One stemming from one decision, another from the alternate.”

“I’ve heard of that! But, Gordon, if you could return to the time of decision here and you had it in your power to say, ‘Yes﻿—live!’ or ‘No﻿—die!’ to the alien natives, what would you do?”

“I don’t know. But neither do I think I shall ever be placed in that position. Why do you ask?”

She was twisting her still damp hair into a pony tail and tying it so with a cord. “Because﻿ ﻿… because I feel.﻿ ﻿… No, I can not really put it into words, Gordon. It is that feeling one has on the eve of some important event﻿—anticipation, fear, excitement. You’ll let me go with you tonight, please! I want to see it﻿—not the Hawaika that is, but that other world with another name, the one they saw and knew!”

===Chinese===
「哪怕它意味着让一整个土著种族有一线生机？」她追问。

「那也许就是平行世界了。」罗斯的想象力被这个念头勾了起来。「历史在一个分岔点上裂出两个世界。」见她面露困惑，他又解释，「一个源自这一种抉择，另一个源自相反的那种。」

「这个我听说过！可是戈登，要是你能回到这里做抉择的时刻，而且你手里有权对外星土著说一声『好——活！』或者『不——死！』，你会怎么做？」

「不知道。但我也不觉得自己真会被推到那个位置上。你为什么问这个？」

她把还湿着的头发拧成一条马尾，用绳子扎住。「因为……因为我感觉到了。……不，我真没法说清楚，戈登。就是人在大事前夜的那种感觉——期待、害怕、兴奋。今晚让我跟你们去，求你了！我要亲眼看一看——不是眼下这个哈瓦伊卡，而是另一个名字的另一个世界，他们当年看见过、生活过的那一个！」

===Original===
An instant protest was hot in Ross’s throat, but he had no time to voice it. For Ashe was already nodding.

“All right. But we may have no luck at all. Fishing in time is a chancy thing, so don’t be disappointed if we don’t turn you up that other world. Now, I’m going to pamper these old bones for an hour or two. Amuse yourselves, children.” He lay back and closed his eyes.

The past two days had wiped half the shadows from his lean, tanned face. He had dropped two years, three, Ross thought thankfully. Let them be lucky tonight, and Ashe’s cure could be nearly complete.

“What do you think happened here?” Karara had moved so that her back was now to the wash of waves, her face more in the shadow.

===Chinese===
一句抗议火辣辣地涌到罗斯喉咙口，却来不及出口。阿什已经在点头了。

「好吧。不过我们也许压根儿钓不着什么。在时间里钓鱼是碰运气的事，要是没能给你捞出那个世界来，可别失望。现在，我要让这把老骨头舒服一两个钟头。你们自己玩去吧，孩子们。」他往后一躺，合上了眼。

过去这两天，已经抹去了他那张瘦削、晒黑的脸上一半的阴影。他年轻了两岁，三岁，罗斯欣慰地想。今晚若能交上好运，阿什的疗养几乎就大功告成了。

「你觉得这里出过什么事？」卡拉拉挪了挪，背对着翻涌的浪，脸埋进了更深的阴影里。

===Original===
“How do I know? Could be any of ten different things.”

“And will I please shut up and leave you alone?” she countered swiftly. “Do you wish to savor the excitement then, explore a world upon world, or am I saying it right? We have Hawaika One which is a new world for us; now there is Hawaika Two which is removed in time, not distance. And to explore that﻿—”

“We won’t be exploring it really,” Ross protested.

“Why? Did your agents not spend days, weeks, even months of time in the past on Terra? What is to prevent your doing the same here?”

“Training. We have no way of learning the drill.”

===Chinese===
「我哪知道？十种八种可能都有。」

「那我是不是该识趣闭嘴，别打扰您老人家？」她立刻反唇相讥，「这么说，你是想细细咂摸这份刺激，去探索一个叠着一个的世界——我这么说对吗？我们有哈瓦伊卡一号，那是我们的新世界；现在又有了哈瓦伊卡二号，它与前者相隔的不是距离，而是时间。要去探索它——」

「其实我们谈不上探索它。」罗斯抗议道。

「为什么？你们的特工在特拉的过去，不是一待就是几天、几星期、甚至几个月吗？有什么能拦着你们在这儿照办？」

「训练。我们没法在这儿演练那一套。」

===Original===
“What do you mean?”

“Well, it wasn’t as easy as you seem to think it was back on Terra,” he began scornfully. “We didn’t just stroll through one of those gates and set up business, say, in Nero’s Rome or Montezuma’s Mexico. An Agent was physically and psychologically fitted to the era he was to explore. Then he trained, and how he trained!” Ross remembered the weary hours spent learning how to use a bronze sword, the technique of Beaker trading, the hypnotic instruction in a language which was already dead centuries before his own country existed. “You learned the language, the customs, everything you could about your time and your cover. You were letter perfect before you took even a trial run!”

“And here you would have no guides,” Karara said, nodding. “Yes, I can see the difficulty. Then you will just use the peep-probe?”

“Probably. Oh, maybe later on we can scout through a gate. We have the material to set one up. But it would be a strictly limited project, allowing no chance of being caught. Maybe the big brains back home can take peep-data and work out some basis of infiltration for us from it.”

“But that would take years!”

===Chinese===
「这话怎么说？」

「哼，在特拉那会儿，可不像你想的那么轻巧。」他不无讥诮地开了腔，「我们不是随便穿过一道那样的门，就能在某处开张营业的——比如说，尼禄的罗马，或者蒙提祖马的墨西哥。特工必须从体格到心理都贴合他要探索的时代。然后是训练，那叫一个训练法！罗斯记得那些疲惫不堪的时辰：学怎么使一柄青铜剑，学比克人的生意经，再在催眠中学习一门语言——那语言在他自己的国家立国之前几百年就死了。「你得学会语言、风俗，把你的时代和身份底细能学的全学到家。连试跑一趟之前，都得做到一字不差！」」

「而在这里你们没有向导。」卡拉拉点点头，「是，我看得出难处了。那你们就只能用窥视探头了？」

「多半是。噢，往后没准也能穿一道门进去侦察。我们有搭门的材料。但那会是个严格受限的行动，绝不容许被抓的可能。没准后方那些大脑子能拿窥视数据当底子，替我们琢磨出一套潜入的方案来。」

「可那得花上好几年！」

===Original===
“I suppose so. Only you begin to swim in the shallows, don’t you﻿—not by jumping off a cliff!”

She laughed. “True enough! However, even a look into the past might solve part of the big mystery.”

Ross grunted and stretched out to follow Ashe’s example. But behind his closed eyes his brain was busy, and he did not cultivate the patience he needed. Peep-probes were all right, but Karara had a point. You wanted more than a small window into a mystery, you wanted a part in solving it.

The setting of the sun deepened rose to red, made a dripping wine-hued banner of most of the sky, so that under it they moved in a crimson sea, looked back at an island where shadows were embers instead of ashes. Three humans, two dolphins, and a machine mounted on a reef which might not even have existed in the time they sought. Ashe made his final adjustments, and then his finger pressed a button and they watched the vista-plate no larger than the palms of two hands.

===Chinese===
「我想是吧。可人学游泳总从浅水开始，不是吗——总不能从悬崖上往下跳！」

她笑了。「这话在理！不过，哪怕只是往过去里看上一眼，兴许也能解开大谜团的一角。」

罗斯哼了一声，学着阿什的样子伸展四肢躺下。可他合上的眼皮后面，脑子转个不停，养不出他需要的那份耐心。窥视探头不是不行，可卡拉拉说得有理。面对一个谜，你不会满足于一扇小小的窗户，你要的是亲手解谜的一份。

落日把玫瑰色加深成了红，把大半天空调成一面淋漓的酒色大纛，于是他们在一派绛红的海里游动，回头望向一座岛，岛上的阴影是余烬，不是死灰。三个人类、两只海豚，还有一台架在礁盘上的机器——在他们要搜寻的那个年代，这礁盘兴许还不存在。阿什做完最后的调校，手指按下一个按钮，他们便盯住了那块不比两只手掌大的全景屏。

===Original===
Nothing, a dull gray nothing! Something must have gone wrong with their assembly work. Ross touched Ashe’s shoulder. But now there were shadows gathering on the plate, thickening, to sharpen into a distinct picture.

It was still the sunset hour they watched. But somehow the colors were paler, less red and sullen than the ones about them in the here and now. And they were not seeing the isle toward which the probe had been aimed; they were looking at a rugged coastline where cliffs lifted well above the beach-strand. While on those cliffs﻿—! Ross had not realized Karara had reached out to grasp his arm until her nails bit into his flesh. And even then he was hardly aware of the pain. Because there was a building on the cliff!

Massive walls of native rock reared in outward defenses, culminating in towers. And from the high point of one tower the pointed tail of a banner cracked in the wind. There was a headland of rock reaching out, not toward them but to the north, and rounding that.﻿ ﻿…

“War canoe!” Karara exclaimed, but Ross had another identification:

===Chinese===
什么也没有，灰蒙蒙的一片空无！准是装配出了岔子。罗斯碰了碰阿什的肩。可就在这时，屏上聚起了影子，越积越浓，最后凝成一幅清晰的画面。

画面上仍是日落时分。但不知怎的，色彩更淡，不像他们此时此地四周的色调那样红得沉郁。探头本是对准那座小岛的，可他们看到的却不是它；他们看到的是一段崎岖的海岸线，崖壁高高拔出滩涂。而在那些崖壁上——！卡拉拉伸手抓住他的手臂，罗斯竟毫无察觉，直到她的指甲掐进他肉里。即便那时，他也几乎不觉得疼。因为崖上有一座建筑！

厚重的墙体用当地岩石垒起，如外张的防御层层拔高，尽处收作塔楼。一面旗帜的尖尾从其中一座塔楼的高点迎风炸响。一道石岬伸向海中——不是朝着他们，而是向北——有什么正绕过那道岬角。……

「战用独木舟！」卡拉拉脱口喊道，罗斯却另有判断：

===Original===
“Longboat!”

In reality, the vessel was neither one nor the other, not the double canoe of the Pacific which had transported warriors on raid from one island to another, or the shield-hung warship of the Vikings. But the Terrans were right in its purpose: That rakish, sharp-prowed ship had been fashioned for swift passage of the seas, for maneuverability as a weapon.

Behind the first nosed another and a third. Their sails were dyed by the sun, but there were devices painted on them, and the lines of those designs glittered as if they had been drawn with a metallic fluid.

“The castle!” Ashe’s cry pulled their attention back to land.

There was movement along those walls. Then came a flash, a splash in the water close enough to the lead ship to wet her deck with spray.

===Chinese===
「维京长船！」

说实话，那船两样都不是——既不是太平洋上那种载着武士跨岛劫掠的双体独木舟，也不是维京人挂满盾牌的战船。但论用途，两个特兰人都猜对了：那艘造型剽悍、船首尖利的船，就是为飞速横渡海洋而造、为当作一件武器般机动而生的。

头船后面又探出第二艘、第三艘。船帆被日头晒得发黄，帆上却画着纹样，那些图样的线条闪闪发亮，仿佛是用金属液描绘出来的。

「城堡！」阿什一声喊，把他们的注意力拽回陆地。

城墙沿线有动静。接着一道闪光，水中溅起一柱浪，离领头船近得飞沫都打湿了它的甲板。

===Original===
“They’re fighting!” Karara shouldered against Ross for a better look.

The ships were altering course, swinging away from land, out to sea.

“Moving too fast for sails alone, and I don’t see any oars.” Ross was puzzled. “How do you suppose.﻿ ﻿…”

The bombardment from the castle continued but did not score any hits. Already the ships were out of range, the lead vessel off the screen of the peep as well. Then there was just the castle in the sunset. Ashe straightened up.

“Rocks!” he repeated wonderingly. “They were throwing rocks!”

===Chinese===
「打起来了！」卡拉拉挤靠着罗斯，想看得更清楚。

船队正在改向，摆离陆地，驶向外海。

「光靠帆走不了这么快，又没见着桨。」罗斯满腹狐疑。「你说他们靠的是什……」

城堡的炮击还在继续，却无一命中。船队已然脱出射程，领头的船也驶出了窥视屏的画面。屏上只剩下落日中的城堡。阿什直起身。

「石弹！」他又惊又疑地念叨，「他们在扔石头！」

===Original===
“But those ships, they must have had engines. They weren’t just depending on sails when they retreated.” Ross added his own cause for bewilderment.

Karara looked from one to the other. “There is something here you do not understand. What is wrong?”

“Catapults, yes,” Ashe said with a nod. “Those would fit periods corresponding from the Roman Empire into the Middle Ages. But you’re right, Ross, those ships had power of some kind to take them offshore that quickly.”

“A technically advanced race coming up against a more backward one?” hazarded the younger man.

“Could be. Let’s go forward some.” The incoming tide was washing well up on the reef. Ashe had to don his mask as he plunged head and shoulders under water to make the necessary adjustment.

===Chinese===
「可那些船，肯定装着发动机。他们撤退时靠的可不光是帆。」罗斯也道出了自己犯迷糊的地方。

卡拉拉看看这个，又看看那个。「你们有想不通的地方。到底哪里不对劲？」

「投石机，没错。」阿什点头道，「这种东西配得上从罗马帝国到中世纪的年代。不过你说得对，罗斯，那些船有某种动力，才能那么快就驶离海岸。」

「一个技术先进的种族，撞上了一个更落后的？」年轻人试着猜。

「有可能。咱们往前挪挪。」涨潮的海水已漫上礁盘。阿什不得不戴上面罩，头肩扎进水里，去做必要的调校。

===Original===
Once more he pressed the button. And Ross’s gasp was echoed by one from the girl. The cliff again, but there was no castle dominating it, only a ruin, hardly more than rubble. Now, above the sites of the saucer depressions great pylons of silvery metal, warmed into fire brilliance by the sunset, raked into the sky like gaunt, skeleton fingers. There were no ships, no signs of any life. Even the vegetation which had showed on shore had vanished. There was an atmosphere of stark abandonment and death which struck the Terrans forcibly.

Those pylons, Ross studied them. Something familiar in their construction teased his memory. That refuel planet where the derelict ship had set down twice, on the voyage out and on their return. That had been a world of metal structures, and he believed he could trace a kinship between his memory of those and these pylons. Surely they had no connection with the earlier castle on the cliff.

Once more Ashe ducked to reset the probe. And in the fast-fading light they watched a third and last picture. But now they might have been looking at the island of the present, save that it bore no vegetation and there was a rawness about it, a sharpness of rock outline now vanished.

Those pylons, were they the key to the change which had come upon this world? What were they? Who had set them there? For the last Ross thought he had an answer. They were certainly the product of the galactic empire. And the castle﻿ ﻿… the ships﻿ ﻿… natives﻿ ﻿… settlers? Two widely different eras, and the mystery still, lay between them. Would they ever be able to bring the key to it out of time?

They swam for the shore where Ui had a fire blazing and their supper prepared.

===Chinese===
他再次按下按钮。罗斯的一声抽气，立刻引来了姑娘同样的一声。又是那道崖，可崖上再没有睥睨四方的城堡，只剩一片废墟，几乎就是瓦砾堆。而此刻，在那几处碟坑遗址的上方，一根根银色金属的巨柱被落日烘出火焰般的光泽，如嶙峋的骸骨之指，直搠天空。没有船，没有任何生命的迹象。连先前岸上还看得到的植被也不见了。一股赤裸裸的弃绝与死亡之气，重重撞在特兰人心头。

那些塔柱，罗斯细细端详。它们的构造里有一种熟稔的东西撩拨着他的记忆。那颗补给燃料的行星——废船曾两度降落在那里，一次在出航时，一次在归途中。那是个遍布金属构筑物的世界，他相信自己能从对那些构筑物的记忆与眼前这些塔柱之间，理出一条亲缘的线索。它们肯定与先前崖上那座城堡无关。

阿什又扎进水里，把探头再调一档。趁着迅速消逝的天光，他们看了第三幅、也是最后一幅画面。这回屏上的几乎就是如今这座岛，只是寸草不生，透着一股毛糙的生涩，岩石轮廓的棱角也比现在锋利。

那些塔柱，会不会就是这个世界巨变的关键？它们是什么？是谁立在那里的？最后一问，罗斯自认有答案：它们必是银河帝国的造物。而那座城堡……那些船……土著……殖民者？两个相去悬殊的时代，谜团依旧横亘其间。他们到底能不能把那把钥匙，从时间里取出来？

他们朝岸边游去，乌伊已在那里燃起一堆火，备好了晚饭。

===Original===
“How many years lying between those probes?” Ross pulled broiled fish apart with his fingers.

“That first was ten thousand years ago, the second,” Ashe paused, “only two hundred years later.”

“But”﻿—Ross stared at his superior﻿—“that means﻿—”

“That there was a war or some drastic form of invasion, yes.”

“You mean that the star people arrived and just took over this whole planet?” Karara asked. “But why? And those pylons, what were they for? How much later was that last picture?”

“Five hundred years.”

===Chinese===
「这两档探测之间隔了多少年？」罗斯用手拆着烤鱼。

「第一幅是一万年前，第二幅嘛，」阿什顿了顿，「只晚两百年。」

「可是」——罗斯瞪着他的上司——「那就是说——」

「就是说这里打过一场战争，或是某种烈性的入侵，没错。」

「你是说，星外来客一到，就整个接管了这颗行星？」卡拉拉问，「可为什么？还有那些塔柱，是做什么用的？最后一幅画面又是多少年之后？」

「五百年后。」

===Original===
“The pylons were gone, too, then,” Ross commented. “But why﻿—?” he echoed Karara’s question.

Ashe had taken up his notebook, but he did not open it. “I think”﻿—there was a sharp, grim note in his voice﻿—“we had better find out.”

“Put up a gate?”

Ashe broke all the previous rules of their service with his answer:

“Yes, a gate.”

===Chinese===
「那时连塔柱也没了。」罗斯说，「可为什么——？」他把卡拉拉的问题又问了一遍。

阿什拿起了笔记本，却没有翻开。「我想」——他的嗓音里有一线尖利的严峻——「我们最好弄个明白。」

「架一道时间之门？」

阿什的回答，破了他们这一行的全部旧规：

「对，一道门。」

## IV Storm Menace / IV 风暴之胁

===Original===
“We have to know.” Ashe leaned back against the crate they had just emptied. “Something was done here﻿—in two hundred years﻿—and then, an empty world.”

“Pandora’s box.” Ross drew a hand across his forehead, smearing sweat and fine sand into a brand.

Ashe nodded. “Maybe we run that risk, loosing all the devils of the aliens. But what if the Reds open the box first on one of their settlement worlds?”

There it was again, the old thorn which prodded them into risks and recklessness. Danger ahead on both paths. Don’t risk trying to learn galactic secrets, but don’t risk your enemy’s learning them either. You held a white-hot iron in both hands in this business. And Ashe was right, they had stumbled on something here which hinted that a whole world had been altered to suit some plan. Suppose the secret of that alteration was discovered by their enemies?

===Chinese===
「我们必须知道。」阿什向后靠在刚倒空的板条箱上。「这里发生过什么——两百年间——然后就只剩一个空荡荡的世界。」

「潘多拉的盒子。」罗斯抹了把额头，把汗水和细沙糊成一道印子。

阿什点头。「也许我们冒的就是这个险——放出外星人的全部妖魔。可要是红方抢先在他们哪颗殖民星球上打开盒子呢？」

又是它——那根老刺，扎得他们去冒险、去孤注一掷。两条路前面都是险。探银河之秘是险，可放任敌人去探同样是险。干这一行，等于双手各攥一块烧白的烙铁。阿什说得对，他们在这儿撞见的东西暗示着：整整一颗世界被改造过，以合于某个计划。倘若那改造的秘密被敌人先发现了呢？

===Original===
“Were the ship and castle people natives?” Ross wondered aloud.

“Just at a guess they were, or at least settlers who had been established here so long they had developed a local form of civilization which was about on the level of a feudal society.”

“You mean because of the castle and the rock bombardment. But what about the ships?”

“Two separate phases of a society at war, perhaps a more progressive against a less technically advanced. American warships paying a visit to the Shogun’s Japan, for example.”

Ross grinned. “Those warships didn’t seem to fancy their welcome. They steered out to sea fast enough when the rocks began to fall.”

===Chinese===
「船上的人和城堡里的人，是土著吗？」罗斯出声寻思。

「姑妄猜之，是土著，或者至少是定居得太久远的殖民者，久到已经演化出一套本地形态的文明，大约处在封建社会的水平。」

「你是说，凭那座城堡和扔石弹。可那些船又怎么讲？」

「也许是同一个社会交战的两副面孔，一边更先进些，另一边技术落后些。好比美国军舰登门拜访幕府的日本。」

罗斯咧嘴一笑。「那支舰队好像不太领受这份欢迎礼。石弹一落，它们溜出外海的速度可真快。」

===Original===
“Yes, but the ships could exist in the castle pattern; the pylons could not!”

“Which period are you aiming for first﻿—the castle or the pylons?”

“Castle first, I think. Then if we can’t pick up any hints, we’ll take some jumps forward until we do connect. Only we’ll be under severe handicaps. If we could only plant an analyzer somewhere in the castle as a beginning.”

Ross did not show his surprise. If Ashe was talking on those terms, then he was intending to do more than just lurk around a little beyond the gate; he was really planning to pick up alien speech patterns, eventually assume an alien agent identity!

“Gordon!” Karara appeared between two of the lace trees. She came so hastily that the contents of the two cups she carried slopped over. “You must hear what Hori has to say﻿—”

===Chinese===
「不错。可那种船放得进城堡的格局里，塔柱却放不进！」

「你先瞄准哪一段——城堡，还是塔柱？」

「我想先奔城堡。要是抓不到线索，就往前跳几档，直到接上线。只是我们将处处受制。要是能在城堡里哪个角落先埋下一台分析器就好了。」

罗斯没有把惊讶露出来。阿什既然这么讲，那就是打算不止在门那头转悠转悠了；他是真要学外星语言的腔调，最终顶上一个外星特工的身份！

「戈登！」卡拉拉从两棵蕾丝树之间现身。她来得急，端着的两只杯子里的东西都晃了出来。「你必须听听霍里怎么说——」

===Original===
The tall Samoan who trailed her spoke quickly. For the first time since Ross had known him he was very serious, a frown line between his eyes. “There is a bad storm coming. Our instruments register it.”

“How long away?” Ashe was on his feet.

“A day﻿ ﻿… maybe two.﻿ ﻿…”

Ross could see no change in the sky, islands, or sea. They had had idyllic weather for the six weeks since their planeting, no sign of any such trouble in the Hawaikan paradise.

“It’s coming,” Hori repeated.

===Chinese===
跟在她身后那个高个子萨摩亚人语速很快。罗斯认识他以来，头一回见他如此郑重，眉心拧着一道纹。「有一场恶风暴要来了。我们的仪器测到了。」

「还有多久？」阿什已站起身。

「一天……也许两天。……」

罗斯看不出天空、岛屿或海面有任何变化。自从降落，他们在这片哈瓦伊卡乐园里过了六个星期田园诗般的日子，从没见过这等祸事的征兆。

「它就要来了。」霍里又说了一遍。

===Original===
“The gate is half up,” Ashe thought aloud, “too much of it set to be dismantled again in a hurry.”

“If it’s completed,” Hori wanted to know, “would it ride out a storm?”

“It might, behind that reef where we have it based. To finish it would be a fast job.”

Hori flexed his hands. “We’re more brawn than brain in these matters, Gordon, but you’ve all our help, for what it’s worth. What about the ship, does it lift on schedule?”

“Check with Rimbault about that. This storm, how will it compare to a Pacific typhoon?”

===Chinese===
「时间之门已经立起一半了，」阿什出声道，「装好的太多，一时半会儿拆不下来。」

「要是装完呢？」霍里想知道，「它扛得住风暴吗？」

「也许扛得住，它就靠着那片礁盘。收尾是很快的活儿。」

霍里活动了一下手指。「这种事我们有力气没脑力，戈登，但有用得着的地方尽管使唤。飞船呢，还按原计划起飞吗？」

「这个去问兰博（Rimbault）。还有这场风暴，跟太平洋台风比怎么样？」

===Original===
The Samoan shook his head. “How do we know? We have not yet had to face the local variety.”

“The islands are low,” Karara commented. “Winds and water could﻿—”

“Yes! We’d better see Rimbault about a shelter if needed.”

If the settlement had drowsed, now its inhabitants were busy. It was decided that they could shelter in the spaceship should the storm reach hurricane proportions, but before its coming the gate must be finished. The final fitting was left to Ashe and Ross, and the older agent fastened the last bolt when the waters beyond the reef were already wind ruffled, the sky darkening fast. The dolphins swam back and forth in the lagoon and with them Karara, though Ashe had twice waved her to the shore.

There was no sunlight left, and they worked with torches. Ashe began his inspection of the relatively simple transfer﻿—the two upright bars, the slab of opaque material forming a doorstep between them. This was only a skeleton of the gates Ross had used in the past. But continual experimentation had produced this more easily transported installation.

===Chinese===
萨摩亚人摇了摇头。「我们怎么知道？本地的这种货色，我们还没领教过。」

「这些岛都很低。」卡拉拉说，「风和水说不定会——」

「对！我们最好去找兰博商量个避风的去处，以防万一。」

殖民点若是先前还在打盹，如今居民们全都忙了起来。议定若风暴刮到飓风的量级，他们可以进飞船里躲避，但在风暴来临之前，时间之门必须完工。收尾安装交给了阿什和罗斯，年长的那位特工拧上最后一颗螺栓时，礁外的水面已被风揉皱，天色暗得飞快。海豚在潟湖里来回游动，卡拉拉陪着它们，尽管阿什已经两次挥手赶她上岸。

太阳已经落尽，他们打着手电干活。阿什开始检查这台构造相对简单的传送装置——两根立柱，中间一块不透明的板，像一道门槛。这不过是罗斯当年用过的那些时间之门的骨架。但持续不断的实验，造出了这套更易搬运的设备。

===Original===
